# Ideas that survived review (2026-11-18 overnight run)

112 of 149 distinct ideas survived at least two of three skeptical reviewers (compliance, novelty, economics).


## Consent Archaeology: the loan officer's own message history as evidence, read on their own device (round 1, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**On day one, the loan officer's own text and email history is read in their browser. It finds every old 'stop' so those people are blocked immediately, and every message the borrower started or opted into, which is kept as dated evidence. The raw messages never leave the device.**

- **Problem:** D2 imports everyone as 'relationship only' (correctly), so on day one a book of 400 past clients has nothing M8 can lawfully text. The book is also hiding the most dangerous fact in it: people who told the loan officer 'stop texting me' years ago on a personal phone. Revocation by any reasonable means stays in force (FCC 24-24, effective 2025-04-11). D4's opt-out list only knows about stops received through Twilio. Loan officers also hold years of off-channel texts with borrowers that the sponsor may have to keep as records but never captured.
- **How it works:** DATA: the loan officer exports their own message history using common tools: Android 'SMS Backup & Restore' XML, an iPhone backup exported to CSV by a desktop tool, Google Takeout (Gmail mbox, Google Voice) or an Outlook export. TRIGGER: step 4 of the import wizard, 'Check your old messages (optional)'. SYSTEM: (1) A sandboxed page with network access blocked (CSP connect-src 'none', the same pattern as Zero-Upload Book Check) parses the file in a Web Worker. It matches threads to D2 contacts by E.164 phone or email. (2) A rules lexicon flags revocations, reusing and extending D4's opt-out words ('stop', 'wrong number', 'don't text me', 'take me off'). It also flags borrower-started threads and explicit invitations ('text me anytime', 'yes you can text me'). (3) Only the flagged snippet goes to the server: up to about 280 characters around the phrase, redacted by lib/m8 redact, with a timestamp, direction, the contact id and a SHA-256 of the full message so the evidence can be checked later. Nothing else is uploaded. (4) Each revocation goes straight into cc_optouts and revokes every phone channel and purpose at once, the same as a live STOP. Each opt-in snippet attaches to the consent log as kind 'historical_evidence' with a strength class: borrower_initiated_thread, informal_invite or explicit_written_optin. (5) Optionally, a sponsor can import full threads into the conversation timeline as retained business records, under the sponsor's own retention policy and only with the loan officer's per-file confirmation. LOAN OFFICER: sees 'Found 14 old opt-outs (now blocked), 61 people who texted you first, 9 written opt-ins to review', approves or rejects each one, and can never upgrade one to marketing consent. BORROWER: notices nothing, except that someone who said stop in 2022 is never contacted again.
- **Technologies:** Browser-side parsing in Web Workers (XML, CSV, mbox), Sandboxed iframe with CSP connect-src 'none', D4 opt-out lexicon plus a phrase classifier (rules first; an optional AI pass on redacted snippets only, with zero retention), SHA-256 evidence hashing, lib/m8 redaction, Neon append-only consents and cc_optouts, The D3 send gate
- **Why it's new:** CRM importers (Bonzo, Total Expert, BNTouch switching guides in the repo research) map contact fields. Reactivation vendors (Structurely, Shape, Flair at West Capital) dial aged lists and assume consent exists. I know of no mortgage CRM that mines the loan officer's own message history for past revocations, or that keeps borrower-started messages as evidence while refusing to treat them as marketing consent. Consent certificates (TrustedForm, Jornaya) belong to lead sellers and record web sessions. These claims rest on repo research and my own knowledge. Competitor features were not re-checked this session because the web search budget was used up.
- **Compliance design:** TCPA: historical evidence never satisfies prior express written consent (47 CFR 64.1200(f)(9) needs a signed agreement with the required disclosures). It can support at most service or informational replies on the thread's topic, and only after counsel sets which strength classes count. Marketing still needs the consent passport e-signature. Any revocation found is applied everywhere and permanently: the strict reading, because the person may have revoked consent for the loan officer, not just their old company. Virginia's 10-year STOP memory is covered automatically because opt-outs never expire. Non-solicit: contacts on a prior-employer hold (D2) stay on hold. Evidence can make the block stronger but never weaker. GLBA and privacy: raw messages never leave the device. Snippets are redacted, and the export is never uploaded. Third-party content in group threads is dropped. The loan officer is a party to every message, so there is no wiretap issue [COUNSEL: confirm for state all-party consent statutes as applied to stored messages]. Fair lending: snippets are evidence only, never ranking inputs, and the Relationship Memory protected-trait filter runs on any AI pass. AI disclosure: not applicable, because nothing is sent. [COUNSEL: which evidence classes, if any, support service texts; sponsor record-retention scope for imported off-channel texts]
- **Moat:** Every rejected or approved classification, aggregated as phrase patterns only and never as messages, improves the shared revocation lexicon for every member. The opt-out list behind One Stop grows with each book imported, so later imports are safer. A data vendor or lead-gen platform has no incentive to build a tool that removes reachable contacts. An MLO-owned platform does.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: Using found revocations to block is lawful and only tightens. Two real risks remain. (1) The 'historical_evidence' classes could drift into being treated as consent. A borrower-initiated thread or 'text me anytime' is not prior express written consent under 47 CFR 64.1200(f)(9), because the required disclosures were never given. At most it is prior express consent for informational messages on that topic, and at worst it is nothing. (2) The exports may be the prior employer's business records (a company phone, or a Workspace Gmail in Takeout). Uploading snippets, and especially the optional full-thread import, can raise trade-secret and record-ownership claims and conflict with the old sponsor's record-keeping. Pooling phrase patterns from message content across tenants also needs a de-identification standard. Note: no fresh legal check was possible this session (search budget used up; ecfr and state statute sites blocked), so citations are from training knowledge. Redesign: Make revocations the only class that acts automatically. Keep opt-in snippets as defense evidence only: they never unlock a channel or purpose, even service texts, until counsel publishes a rule. Block the full-thread import, and leave snippets local-only, for any export the LO tags as from a prior-employer device or account, or whose sender domain matches a D2 prior-employer hold. Upload only phrase-lexicon features that pass a k-anonymity threshold and contain no names or numbers. / keep 7/10: I know of no CRM or reactivation vendor that mines an LO's own message history for past revocations. TrustedForm and Jornaya certify web sessions for lead sellers, which is a different job. Weak points: iPhone export depends on third-party desktop tools, and the evidence classes only matter once counsel defines them. I could not re-verify this: the shared web-search budget was used up, so every verdict here rests on repo research and training knowledge. Redesign: Ship the revocation finder first: it only tightens consent and is safe whatever counsel decides. Hold opt-in evidence classes until counsel rules. Make it step 4 of D2 import. / keep 5/10: The data is thinner than the pitch. Android 'SMS Backup & Restore' XML and Google Takeout (Gmail mbox, Google Voice) are free and parseable. iPhones, which are most US MLO phones, need a paid desktop tool such as iMazing or iExplorer, and the CSV formats differ by tool. MLOs coming from large lenders usually texted through the employer's platform (Velocify, Bonzo, Total Expert), and that history is prior-employer data they can't take. It runs once at onboarding, not weekly. The opt-in evidence classes add little because they can never become marketing consent. The real value is finding old STOPs, and for a typical personal book that is a few dozen rows. It is still cheap, because D2's in-browser parser and sandbox already exist, and it is a strong trust signal for recruits. These are training-knowledge facts: the web search budget was used up and outside hosts were blocked this session. Redesign: Ship v1 for revocation finding only, with two parsers: Android XML and Google Takeout (mbox and Voice). Feed hits straight into cc_optouts. Leave out the 'historical_evidence' consent classes until counsel says a class supports service texts. Add iPhone through one named export tool's CSV later. Budget about 1.5 weeks and fold it into the D2 import wizard as an optional step.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md (D2 consent log and holds; D4 cc_optouts and opt-out words), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (Zero-Upload Book Check pattern; One Stop), https://kelleydrye.com/viewpoints/blogs/ad-law-access/fcc-sets-effective-date-for-tcpa-reasonable-consent-revocation-rules, https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-64/subpart-L/section-64.1200 (canonical; not opened, host blocked this session), https://www.privacyworld.blog/2026/01/key-changes-to-virginia-telephone-privacy-protection-act-take-effect/, https://www.housingwire.com/articles/west-capital-flair-ai-voice/, https://www.structurely.com/news/insights/how-mortgage-teams-can-reactivate-dormant-leads-with-ai-powered-outreach, Verification note: the web search budget was exhausted and ecfr.gov and law.cornell.edu were outside the allowed network, so legal points are from training knowledge and repo sources


## Pass-Along Card: past-client referrals that never collect a friend's number (round 1, high impact, ~3 weeks)

**A happy past client forwards a personal card from their own phone. The friend starts the conversation and gives their own consent. The referrer learns nothing about the friend unless the friend chooses to tell them. There is no reward, only a handwritten thank-you.**

- **Problem:** Referrals from past clients are the cheapest funded loans in a book, but the standard CRM 'refer a friend' form is a legal trap. The referrer types in the friend's phone number, and the platform texts or calls someone who never consented. Under the TCPA, consent must come from the person called: the subscriber or customary user. The FCC's GroupMe ruling (2014) allows consent passed through an intermediary but leaves the caller liable if the person never actually gave it. Reward programs add RESPA Section 8 and state inducement risk. Telling the referrer 'Sam applied!' discloses Sam's nonpublic personal information.
- **How it works:** DATA: closed loans (POS or D2 'own closings'), post-close moments, M8 call intent tag 'referral offered', and 'referred by' text already in imported notes and LOS referral fields. TRIGGER: (a) the borrower says something like 'my sister is looking' to M8 or the loan officer; (b) the post-close day-30 check-in answer is positive; (c) the loan officer taps 'Offer a pass-along card'. SYSTEM: creates a per-referrer link (/p/{code}) with an optional line the referrer writes ('Jason got us through a crazy appraisal'). It opens their phone's own share sheet (Web Share API) or a copy button. The platform never asks for, accepts or stores the friend's contact details: the form has no field for them, and the server rejects any. The friend lands in M8 chat. The AI disclosure comes first, then routing by property state (lib/routing.ts; if the loan officer isn't licensed there, the routing or Relay flow applies honestly). The friend sets their own consent through the consent passport. At the end, M8 asks the friend, 'Want us to let Dana know you connected? (Nothing about your loan is shared.)' Only a yes produces a thank-you prompt for the loan officer. REFERRAL ROOT FINDER: from imported notes and LOS referral fields, an extractor finds households whose introductions led to closed loans. It shows the loan officer their top consumer connectors (counts only, no amounts), and the ask is always personal. LOAN OFFICER: Today card 'Dana's card was used: a new conversation from Spokane', plus a reminder to send a handwritten note (a printable card, no gift). FRIEND: their own first-party conversation, starting from zero data.
- **Technologies:** Web Share API share sheet, Signed short links, M8 chat with an opener aware of the referrer's note, Consent passport, lib/routing.ts licensing check, A notes extractor (shared with Relationship Memory) for 'referred by' entities, Neon referral_links and introductions tables with no friend PII until the friend gives it, The D3 send gate
- **Why it's new:** From the repo research and my own knowledge (not re-verified this session; search budget exhausted): CRM and agent tools commonly offer referral portals and partner portals where the referrer enters the friend's details. Some lenders run consumer referral reward programs. I know of no mortgage platform built so the friend's number cannot be collected, with consent that starts with the friend and status shared only when the friend opts in. Relay (idea 11) covers moves out of state, not friend introductions. Warm Path covers agents, not consumers.
- **Compliance design:** TCPA: no outbound touch to the friend ever. Every friend contact starts inbound and the friend gives their own consent (FCC 2015 Omnibus Order: consent belongs to the subscriber or customary user; GroupMe 2014: the caller is liable where an intermediary's claim of consent is false). The referrer's own consent and the D3 gate govern any message to the referrer. RESPA 8 and state law: no fee, gift card, credit, closing-cost credit or contest for referrals, enforced by a ledger field that cannot hold value. The thank-you is a handwritten note at the loan officer's own cost, below any value threshold counsel sets. check:copy keeps 'referral fee' and earnings language out [COUNSEL: whether nominal thank-you items are permissible in WA, OR, AZ, CA and TX]. GLBA: the referrer learns nothing about the friend unless the friend opts in. Status never includes application, approval or loan facts. Fair lending: referral networks can mirror existing demographics, so the fair-lending report shows the share of new conversations by source. Pass-Along never replaces general outreach, and the Root Finder ranks only by introduction count, never by area or demographic inputs. AI disclosure is shown before the friend's first M8 turn. No rate outside a priced result (the M8 guard).
- **Moat:** With every member using the same card, a friend who moves or lives in another state reaches a licensed member through routing, not a lead buyer, so the network's coverage makes each card more useful. The extractor learns referral phrasing across books (patterns only). Lead-selling platforms can't copy the 'we never take your friend's number' promise, because their model depends on collecting numbers.
- **Reviews:** keep 9/10: This is the cleanest design in the set. The referrer picks the recipients and sends from their own device through the share sheet. Under the FCC's 2015 Omnibus treatment of app-generated invitations (the Glide and TextMe analysis, from training knowledge), the platform is then not the 'maker' of the message, because it never stores or chooses recipients. RESPA 8 applies to 'any person' who receives value for a referral, and the ledger field that cannot hold value meets it. Remaining risks: (a) a 'printable card' with any enclosure, such as a gift card or a coffee voucher, would be a thing of value, and some states read referral-gift rules strictly; (b) the status ping to the referrer must come only from the friend's own yes, which is consent under Reg P 1016.15(a)(1). Redesign: Hard-code the thank-you as a note with no enclosure. The ledger rejects any amount above $0, and the printable template has no voucher field. Prefill the share-sheet message with only the referrer's own words and the link, and add no platform marketing language, so the message stays the referrer's own. / drop 4/10: Share-link referrals through the phone's share sheet are standard. Consumer lender refer-a-friend programs and Homebot-style share links already ship them. Leaving out the friend field and the reward is a compliance design choice, not a new product. The Referral Root Finder adds little. Redesign: Make it a small feature: a shareable M8 link with the referrer's note and an opt-in 'tell Dana we connected' step. Drop it as a standalone idea. / keep 7/10: This is cheap: a per-referrer code, the Web Share API (supported on iOS and Android browsers), a landing page in M8 chat and routing that already exists. Past-client referrals are the top source of organic business for most MLOs, so a weekly-use path is plausible, and it needs no licensed data. Two weak spots. Attribution leaks whenever the friend calls the MLO directly. The 'Referral Root Finder' depends on free-text notes and LOS referral fields that are often empty or messy, so its output will be noisy. Redesign: Build the card, the share link and the friend's opt-in 'tell Dana' step first (about 1 to 2 weeks). Cut the Root Finder to a simple 'referred by' field count from structured import columns only, with no AI extraction pass. Keep the printable thank-you note with a zero-value ledger field.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md (sections 2.1, 2.3, 5.3), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (ideas 3, 7, 11), https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-15-72A1.pdf (2015 TCPA Omnibus Declaratory Ruling; not opened this session), https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/FCC-14-33A1.pdf (GroupMe declaratory ruling; not opened this session), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1024/2023-04-19/14/, https://www.mayerbrown.com/en/insights/publications/2023/08/respa-enforcement-is-back-the-cfpb-takes-aim-at-marketing-and-promotional-activities, Verification note: FCC ruling citations are from training knowledge; docs.fcc.gov was not reachable and the web search budget was exhausted


## Not-Yet Desk: stalled, withdrawn, denied and expired pre-approval files, re-screened without reusing the credit report (round 1, high impact, ~5 weeks)

**The second-largest asset in every book, applicants who never closed, gets a re-screen against today's products using only non-credit facts, plus a readiness plan the borrower sets, so 'not yet' becomes a fresh, compliant application.**

- **Problem:** Loan officers usually have two to three times as many unclosed files as closings. CRMs treat these as dead leads for drip campaigns, and AI reactivation vendors dial aged lead pools, which is exactly the TCPA exposure behind the 2026 suits. Reusing the original credit report to re-target these people is a hidden FCRA problem. The report was pulled for the application the consumer started (15 U.S.C. 1681b(f) requires a permissible purpose for each use). Re-marketing on its contents works like a prescreen without a firm offer. Uneven follow-up can also become ECOA discouragement (Reg B 1002.4(b)).
- **How it works:** DATA: LOS or CSV export of unclosed files: status (withdrawn, denied, incomplete, pre-approval expired, never found a home), dates, loan purpose, property state, documentation type (W-2, self-employed, 1099, retired), down payment saved as the borrower stated it, the loan officer's stall-reason category, and (for denied files) only the fact of denial and the date, never the report contents or score. Program tables in config: the current product matrix (agency, FHA, VA, USDA, the panel's non-QM bank-statement and DSCR programs) and DPA programs with county income limits and sources. TRIGGER: import, or a nightly re-screen when the product matrix or DPA table changes. SYSTEM: (1) A firewall rule: credit-report fields (tri-merge, scores, tradelines, inquiry data) can't be imported into the desk. The zod schema rejects them, and a CI test checks the columns. (2) A re-screen on non-credit facts only: 'self-employed, stalled on income docs, 2023' matches 'a bank-statement program now exists on your panel', with the QM option always shown first. 'Couldn't find a home under $450k' matches DPA programs whose income limits the borrower can check. (3) Contact windows per file: hand-dialing is allowed inside the 3-month inquiry or application EBR window, or when the number isn't on a DNC list. Email is allowed under CAN-SPAM. M8 and texts need consent. (4) The borrower's readiness plan: the borrower picks their own goals (savings target, documents to gather, a date), optionally connects a bank account to track savings (phase 2), and sets their own check-in dates (shared with Time Capsule). LOAN OFFICER: a 'Not yet' tab on Today ('11 files may fit a program that didn't exist when they stalled'), with a personal script that never mentions why they were denied. BORROWER: 'Want a no-pressure plan for when you're ready?' When they hit their goal or say 'ready', a fresh application starts with a new credit authorization.
- **Technologies:** LOS and CSV connectors (ARIVE status fields), zod schema firewall plus a CI column test, Product-matrix and DPA tables in config with citations and expiry, D3 send gate with EBR windows, Consent passport and Time Capsule scheduling, Optional Plaid Auth or Balance for borrower-chosen savings tracking (phase 2), M8 chat and voice under the guard
- **Why it's new:** Reactivation tools (Structurely, Shape, Total Expert AI Sales Assistant, Flair) work aged leads by dialing, and the repo research found no consent methodology. Rapid-rescore and credit-improvement services are ordered by lenders on a live file. From the repo research and my own knowledge (competitor features not re-verified this session), I found no CRM that re-screens unclosed files against a changed product matrix while technically blocking reuse of the credit report, and hands the 'when' to the borrower.
- **Compliance design:** FCRA: report contents never enter the desk (schema-level block). Any new evaluation needs a new application and authorization. This also keeps it clear of HPPA prescreen issues. ECOA and Reg B: every stalled file in a category gets the same offer, which prevents selective discouragement (1002.4(b)). There's no protected-trait input, and DPA eligibility is checked by income limits the borrower confirms, never by tract demographics. Denied applicants already received adverse action notices, and copy never implies approval. Credit repair: the desk gives free education only, never charges and never promises credit improvement, which keeps it outside the Credit Repair Organizations Act definition [COUNSEL]. Reg Z comp and anti-steering: the QM or agency option always comes first, and non-QM is shown only alongside it, with loan officer pay identical across products. TCPA and DNC: EBR applies only to hand-dialed calls and only within 3 months of inquiry or application (47 CFR 64.1200(f)(5)). Texts and M8 calls need consent, and all touches go through the D3 gate. Retention: Reg B 1002.12 keeps application records 25 months under the sponsor's policy, and the desk honors sponsor holds and deletions. Non-solicit: prior-employer files stay on D2 hold. AI disclosure before every M8 touch, and no rate outside a priced result.
- **Moat:** The product-matrix and DPA tables are maintained once for every member, and members in different states improve coverage. A solo loan officer can't keep a five-state DPA table current. Over time the platform learns which stall categories match which product changes (aggregate counts only), which makes each nightly re-screen sharper.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: The schema-level firewall on report fields is right. But the 'stall-reason category' and the fact of denial can quietly carry consumer-report information: 'credit score too low' or 'DTI from tradelines' are derived from the report. Using report-derived facts for a later solicitation is outside the original permissible purpose (FCRA 604) and is effectively an unscreened prescreen. Two further points. EBR only exempts live, hand-dialed calls from DNC, and the inquiry EBR lasts 3 months, which the idea states correctly. Selective re-contact by 'self-employed' toward non-QM is fine only if QM is presented first and LO pay is identical. For files that came from a prior sponsor, application data is that sponsor's GLBA consumer data, so the D2 hold must cover it. Redesign: Limit stall categories to a fixed list of non-credit reasons: documentation, home search, down payment, withdrew by choice, and timing. Make 'credit-related' a single opaque bucket that triggers only the generic 'want a no-pressure plan?' offer, never a program match. Add a CI test that rejects any stall-category value naming score, tradeline, inquiry or DTI. Apply the D2 prior-sponsor hold to application data, not just contacts. / keep 6/10: Surefire, Total Expert and Jungo already run dead-lead and lost-opportunity journeys, so re-engaging unclosed files is not new. What is new: the schema-level block on credit data, and nightly re-screening against a changed product and DPA matrix. A five-state DPA table is a heavy maintenance load. Redesign: Position it as 'product-change re-screen' and skip the generic reactivation pitch. Start with the panel's non-QM and agency matrix only, and add DPA later per state. / keep 6/10: Unclosed files are real and valuable, but two economics problems remain. (1) Recruits' unclosed files from a prior employer stay on D2 hold, so the desk only covers files created under the current sponsor. Value starts near zero for a new member and builds over 6 to 12 months. (2) A five-state, county-level DPA table is the costly part: there are thousands of DPA programs nationally, and limits change every year. A small team should license that data (Down Payment Resource sells a DPA database to lenders; vendor, recalled and not verified this session) rather than maintain it. The product-matrix re-screen also needs someone to keep panel non-QM eligibility current. Redesign: v1 matches stall-reason categories to program availability from the existing lender panel config, with the QM option first. No DPA table: link the state HFA programs instead. Price a DPA data license before building any table. Keep the zod block on credit fields and the borrower-set readiness plan. Drop phase-2 bank-account tracking.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md (section 2.2: no credit data, fair lending), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md (D2 holds, D3 EBR hand-dial rule), https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1681b (FCRA 604(f); not opened, host blocked this session), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1002/4/ (Reg B discouragement; not opened), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1002/12/ (record retention; not opened), https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1679a (CROA definitions; not opened), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-64/subpart-L/section-64.1200 (EBR definition; not opened), https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/mortgage-tcpa-lawsuits-surge-with-11-new-cases


## Homecoming Routing: the network returns each past client to their own loan officer at every front door (round 1, high impact, ~3 weeks)

**When anyone shows up through LoanM8.com, M8 chat, a future consumer front end, another member's checkup line or a borrower's AI assistant, the platform recognizes a member's past client (with that person's verification) and offers their own loan officer first. It never hides the choice and never sells the visit.**

- **Problem:** Loan officers lose past clients at the front door. The borrower searches, lands on a portal or lead marketplace, and is sold to whoever bids, or is recaptured by a servicer. The repo research found a MloFlo review saying leads went to other loan officers after a cancellation. As Command Center adds consumer front ends (LoanM8 first, others later) and AI assistants start shopping for borrowers, a platform that sends a member's own client to a different member would break the ownership promise. A platform that blocks the borrower's choice would break antitrust and consumer law.
- **How it works:** DATA: each member's past clients from own-closings and personal provenance (D2), registered as salted HMACs of their E.164 phone and normalized email (the One Stop pattern), tied to the member's seat and never visible to other members. TRIGGER: a visitor at any platform entry point gives a phone or email, for example to save a chat or ask for a callback. SYSTEM: (1) Hash lookup. On a match, the platform sends a one-time code to that number or email. The visitor asked to be contacted, so it's an informational, user-initiated message. Only after the code is verified does M8 say: 'Looks like you worked with [loan officer] before. Want to continue with them, or choose someone else?' (2) If the original loan officer isn't licensed in the property state (lib/routing.ts), the Relay flow runs instead. If they've left the platform, their hashes are gone and nothing is said. (3) 'Choose someone else' shows the normal routing (random order, nothing pre-selected). (4) Every decision is logged. No member sees another member's match, and visits are never sold or auctioned. (5) AI assistants (the gateway in the adjacent-tech survey) get the same behavior: the human verifies the code before any loan officer is named. LOAN OFFICER: Today card 'Your 2021 client Priya came back through LoanM8 chat and chose you: refinance question', with the chat transcript under the borrower's consent. BORROWER: is recognized only after proving it's them, and always gets a real choice.
- **Technologies:** Salted HMAC identity registry per member (the One Stop pattern), One-time code verification (Twilio Verify or email), lib/routing.ts and Relay, Consent passport, An MCP or agent gateway hook for AI-assistant traffic, Neon audit log of routing decisions
- **Why it's new:** Portals and marketplaces route consumers to paying lenders. Servicers recapture through their own retention desks (Rocket and Mr. Cooper, ICE MSP agents in the survey). Enterprise CRMs keep the book inside one company. From the repo research and my own knowledge (not re-verified; search exhausted), I found no multi-company platform whose routing recognizes a returning past client, offers their original loan officer first and still guarantees the borrower's free choice. Relay (idea 11) handles moves out of the loan officer's states. One Stop shares opt-outs. This shares neither data nor leads: only a borrower-verified 'welcome back'.
- **Compliance design:** Antitrust: an agreement among competing members not to serve each other's customers would be customer allocation, a serious antitrust risk. So this is a borrower-facing default offer only. Members stay free to compete, the borrower can always choose anyone, and nothing in member terms restricts serving any consumer [COUNSEL: antitrust review of the routing default]. GLBA: the existence of a customer relationship is nonpublic personal information, so it's disclosed only to the verified person themselves, never to other members. Hashes are salted per platform and deleted when the member leaves. TCPA: the verification code is a one-time message the person requested, and nothing marketing-related follows without consent through the D3 gate. RESPA 8: nothing of value moves, and routing is never paid or ranked by payment. Licensing: original loan officers are named only in their licensed states. Fair lending: recognition depends only on the hash match, the same for everyone. AI disclosure precedes M8. State privacy: the hash registry is listed in the privacy notice, with deletion on request.
- **Moat:** It gets stronger with every member and every new front end: the more doors the platform has (LoanM8 and future white-label consumer sites, AI assistants), the more often a member's own past client is returned to them. A lead marketplace can't offer this, because its revenue is selling that visit.
- **Reviews:** keep 6/10: (1) GLBA and state privacy. The hash registry is built from sponsor A's customer NPI and used at doors run by other entities, such as LoanM8 or member B's line. That goes beyond service-provider use (Reg P 1016.11 reuse limits) unless sponsor A's contract permits it. California's Financial Information Privacy Act requires opt-in before sharing with nonaffiliated parties (Cal. Fin. Code 4052.5, from training knowledge; leginfo was unreachable), and the welcome-back message appears in the door owner's transcript. (2) RESPA 8. Members pay the platform and the platform steers inbound consumers to a paying member. The CFPB's 2023 advisory opinion on digital mortgage comparison platforms treated non-neutral steering by a paid operator as a referral-fee risk. Its current status is unverified; it may have been among the guidance withdrawn in 2025, but the statute still applies and states enforce it. (3) Antitrust. Default-to-incumbent at a competitor's own door is close to customer allocation. Redesign: Apply it only at platform-owned doors (LoanM8 and white-label sites whose operator opts in by contract), never at another member's own line. Show the 'welcome back' only after the code is verified, and log the consumer's choice as Reg P 1016.15(a)(1) consent. Keep the recognition text out of the door owner's transcript store. Get sponsor-agreement permission for the registry, and exclude CA and VT customers unless they opted in. Set subscription prices the same whether or not a member receives Homecoming matches, and disclose to consumers that listed LOs pay platform fees. / keep 7/10: Servicer retention desks and single-company CRMs recapture within one company. A cross-company 'welcome back' that the borrower verifies, with free choice preserved, has no known equivalent. It only pays off once there are many front doors and members, and the antitrust question about the routing default is real. Redesign: Build it after there are several front ends and members. Ship it with Choice-First Territories so the antitrust firewall is in place on day one. / drop 4/10: It needs several front doors and many members to fire at all. Today LoanM8 is the only front end with a handful of MLOs, and a past client who wants their MLO usually just texts them. The hash build is cheap, but match rates would be near zero for a year or more. It also carries antitrust review cost (a customer-allocation look) and GLBA notice changes for every sponsor. Real cost now for little near-term benefit. Redesign: Defer until there are two or more consumer front ends and at least 50 members. Meanwhile, a past client who uses LoanM8 chat can type their MLO's name or open their MLO's personal link. That needs no registry.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (One Stop, idea 11 Relay), /home/user/RATEM8/CLAUDE.md (section 4c routing, lib/routing.ts), https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/who-owns-client-data-only-the-contract-knows, https://www.capterra.com/p/212895/Bonzo/ (category review context from the survey), https://www.wsgr.com/en/insights/doj-and-ftc-withdraw-antitrust-guidelines-for-competitor-collaborations.html, https://www.rismedia.com/2026/10/30/rocket-beats-expectations-in-q3-earnings-report-amid-stressed-mortgage-market/, Verification note: the MloFlo review comes from the market survey; the antitrust characterization of customer allocation is from training knowledge, not re-verified this session


## Loan Officer Mobility Protocol: a multi-sponsor agreement, with the software that runs it, for taking your book lawfully (round 1, high impact, ~3 weeks)

**Modeled on the securities industry's Broker Protocol: sponsors on the platform opt in to a reciprocal agreement under which a departing loan officer may take only the names and contact details of clients they personally served, gives the old sponsor a matching list, and announces the move. Command Center computes the eligible list and signs the handoff.**

- **Problem:** For a recruit, day one comes down to which past clients they may lawfully contact. Today that is set by each employer's contract and fought out in court: the poaching suits in the repo research (Griffin v. West Capital, Citizens v. SoFi, Stockton, the CCM non-solicit action), and NEXA rewrote its contracts after recruits objected. D2 puts prior-employer lists on hold, which is safe, but it leaves most of a recruit's book stuck on day one. Mortgage has no equivalent of the protocol that ended much of this litigation for securities brokers.
- **How it works:** AGREEMENT: an opt-in protocol (counsel-drafted) that sponsoring brokerages and lenders on the platform sign, which commits them to: (1) a departing loan officer may take only the name, address, phone and email of clients they personally originated or served; no loan files, documents, credit or loan terms; (2) on the day they leave, they give the old sponsor a copy of exactly that list; (3) after joining the new sponsor they may announce the move and respond to clients; (4) the old sponsor keeps all loan files and servicing contacts and may keep serving the clients; (5) the borrower's choice wins in every case. SOFTWARE: D2 provenance tags (own closings, personal) plus LOS 'originating loan officer' fields compute the eligible list. Prior-employer system files that don't qualify stay on hold. A departure manifest (the list plus a SHA-256 hash) is signed with KMS and delivered to both sponsors, and the D2 hold is released only for listed contacts, only if both sponsors are signatories, and only after the effective date. An announcement linter blocks rate claims, disparagement and missing NMLS or company identifiers under state advertising rules, and limits announcements to email and mail (CAN-SPAM). Texts and calls still need consent through the D3 gate. LOAN OFFICER: 'Your protocol list: 288 clients you originated. Announcement ready; 212 can be emailed today.' OLD SPONSOR: gets the manifest the same day and keeps everything else. BORROWER: gets a plain announcement with an unsubscribe link and the old sponsor's servicing contact.
- **Technologies:** Counsel-drafted multi-party protocol with a signatory registry, D2 provenance tags and holds, LOS originating-loan-officer fields (ARIVE first), KMS-signed departure manifests with hashes, An announcement linter (check:copy rules plus state ad-disclosure rules from config), Resend and a print-mail API, The D3 send gate
- **Why it's new:** In securities, the Protocol for Broker Recruiting (started 2004; joined by over a thousand firms; Morgan Stanley and UBS withdrew in 2017; from training knowledge, not re-verified this session) lets advisors take basic client contact information and solicit after a move, which cut down temporary restraining order litigation. I know of no mortgage equivalent. The market survey's 'seat follows the MLO' and sponsor-handoff ideas describe account mechanics inside one platform. This is the inter-sponsor legal agreement that makes those mechanics enforceable and gives the old sponsor a matching list. Move-In Week covers license transfer, and Book Deed covers export only.
- **Compliance design:** GLBA and Reg P: a customer list is nonpublic personal information, so signatory sponsors update their privacy notices to disclose the protocol, and counsel decides the sharing basis for each sponsor (exceptions versus opt-out notice) [COUNSEL]. Only contact fields move, never loan files or credit data. TCPA: the protocol never creates consent. Announcements go by email or mail only, and texts and M8 calls still need consent through the gate. Antitrust: the protocol increases competition for talent and clients (the opposite of a no-poach agreement), stays voluntary and non-exclusive, and covers no prices or wages [COUNSEL]. Trade secrets and contracts: the protocol works alongside existing non-solicits only where both sponsors have signed. Otherwise D2 holds stay in place, and the system never tells a loan officer they may solicit. RESPA and Reg Z: nothing of value moves between sponsors or to the loan officer for the list. Advertising: NMLS ID and company identifiers per state rules from config/licensing.ts. Fair lending: the eligible list depends only on who served the client.
- **Moat:** A protocol gets more valuable with every signatory: each sponsor that joins makes the platform's sponsors more attractive to recruits and lowers litigation cost for all of them. Because it's tied to the platform's provenance data and signed manifests, it works best on Command Center. A loan officer-owned network is the credible host, since no single large lender would run a protocol that helps its loan officers leave.
- **Reviews:** keep 6/10: The securities Broker Protocol works because Reg S-P privacy notices disclose the sharing and give customers an opt-out. In mortgage, the list of clients the LO served is the old sponsor's customer NPI under Reg P. Handing it to a departing LO and their new, nonaffiliated sponsor needs a revised privacy notice and an opt-out before sharing, under 1016.10. California (FIPA, Cal. Fin. Code 4052.5) and Vermont require affirmative opt-in for nonaffiliated sharing, so the protocol fails there as drafted. Reg P 1016.12 also bars sharing account numbers for marketing. Antitrust risk is low, and the no-poach analysis cuts in its favor. The idea's facts on the Broker Protocol (Morgan Stanley and UBS withdrew in 2017) match training knowledge but were not re-verified. Redesign: Restrict the protocol list in every state to (a) the LO's personal-provenance contacts, which are not sponsor customers, and (b) sponsor customers who were given the revised notice and did not opt out, or in CA and VT who affirmatively opted in. Make the platform refuse to release the D2 hold for a CA or VT customer without an opt-in record. Never include loan numbers or account numbers. / keep 8/10: It is modeled on the securities Broker Protocol, and I know of no mortgage equivalent. It is the strongest recruiting differentiator against large companies. The risk is adoption: it is worthless until enough sponsors sign, and large lenders won't. Redesign: Combine it with Sponsor Commons. Signing the protocol becomes a condition of the Portable Seat Addendum, so a single document seeds both networks. / drop 4/10: Adoption economics are the problem. In securities, the large wirehouses (Morgan Stanley, UBS, Citi) withdrew from the Broker Protocol around 2017 to 2018 (recalled, not verified this session). In mortgage, the large lenders the founder is recruiting from are exactly the firms that won't sign. Small sponsors who would sign rarely litigate over departures anyway. Drafting and maintaining a multi-party agreement is real legal spend. The software side (eligible list from provenance, signed manifest) mostly already exists in D2 provenance and Book Deed. Redesign: Fold it into 'Sponsor Commons with a Portable Seat Addendum' as one clause set, covering contact-only portability, a matching list to the old sponsor and email-only announcements, signed bilaterally between sponsors on the platform. Keep the signed departure manifest as a feature of that addendum.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (idea 1, Move-In Week, Book Deed; poaching case sources), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md (D2 prior-employer holds and attestation), https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/who-owns-client-data-only-the-contract-knows, https://www.housingwire.com/articles/griffin-funding-alleges-west-capital-lending-former-los-diverted-borrower-leads/, https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/citizens-sues-sofi-as-fights-over-talent-pipelines-persist, https://www.housingwire.com/articles/ccm-enforcement-action-non-solicitation/, https://www.housingwire.com/articles/stockton-mortgage-gmail-lawsuit-2/, Verification note: Broker Protocol history and terms are from training knowledge and must be verified before use; the web search budget was exhausted this session


## Partner Room: borrower-directed sharing with every professional on the file (round 1, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**The borrower invites their agent, CPA, financial advisor, attorney or builder into a scoped, expiring, revocable view of their own file. Every partner on a file gets exactly the same tools, nobody pays for a seat, and nothing works without a borrower file.**

- **Problem:** Referral partners are the main source of purchase business, but today an LO shares file facts with them by forwarding emails and texting screenshots. That is a GLBA problem (nonpublic personal information leaves with no logged consent), a RESPA problem (agent portals and co-paid seats can read as a thing of value), and a trust problem: borrowers never see who saw what. Agent Line (ideas doc #8) covers phone verification for agents only. Nothing covers CPAs, advisors, attorneys or builders, who each need different slices of the same file.
- **How it works:** Data: the D2 contact, the D6 loan record, and documents the borrower uploads. Trigger: the borrower taps "Add someone to my team" in M8 chat or the consent-passport page, or the LO sends an invite the borrower approves. What the system does: (1) Verifies the invitee's identity by email or phone link. Where a public license lookup exists, it records license status for the role: the state real estate commission for agents, NASBA's CPAverify for CPAs, the state bar for attorneys. (2) Creates a grant with a role template: agent = milestones and contract dates; CPA = income documents and the income worksheet; advisor = funds-to-close and reserves; attorney = the settlement-term checklist; builder = completion stages for one lot. Each grant also has a field list, a purpose and an expiry (default: closing plus 30 days). (3) Logs every partner read in the GLBA audit log and shows the borrower a "who looked" list with one-tap revoke. (4) An anonymous-first front door: a partner can ask a scenario question with no names ("self-employed, two years of Schedule C, $X averaged, can they buy at $Y?"). The LO answers from a template. Only when the client opts in does a room open. What the MLO does: approves invites and answers the anonymous questions. What the partner experiences: one link with only what the client allowed, no login wall and no marketing. Partner contact details are stored relationship-only, and the send gate blocks any marketing to them unless they separately consent. What the borrower experiences: control, and a visible log.
- **Technologies:** Neon row-level security with a per-grant policy table, D3 send gate (partner contacts stored relationship-only), GLBA audit log (existing cc_audit pattern), Signed magic links (D1 sign-in pattern), Public license lookups (state real estate commissions, CPAverify, state bars) where terms allow, zod-validated role templates, Consent passport (plan 5.3)
- **Why it's new:** Agent portals in LOS and POS tools (Floify's third-party recipient sync, Encompass-driven milestone emails) push status to agents chosen by the lender, with no borrower-side log and no non-agent roles. Homebot's lender-sponsored agent seats are the RESPA-sensitive pattern the market survey flags. The ideas doc's Agent Line is phone-only and agent-only. As far as this survey found, nothing gives one borrower-controlled grant model across agents, CPAs, advisors, attorneys and builders, with license-verified partners and a read log the borrower can see. Verification caveat: web search was exhausted and partner-portal pages couldn't be opened this session, so the competitor gap rests on the repo's prior research plus the market survey.
- **Compliance design:** GLBA: sharing rests on the consumer-consent exception (Reg P, 12 CFR 1016.15(a)(1), "with the consent or at the direction of the consumer"). Grants are field-scoped, expiring, revocable and audited [COUNSEL: confirm for the sponsor's privacy notice]. RESPA 8 and Reg X 1024.14: the room exists only for a borrower's own file, so it serves the borrower and is not a free business tool for the partner. Every partner on a file gets identical features. There are no seats, no tiers and nothing unlocked by number of files. The system never computes or shows "files received from this partner" against "value given to this partner", because a reciprocity score could be evidence of an agreement or understanding (the ideas doc's Relay reasoning). Lawyers' and CPAs' own rules (ABA Model Rule 7.2(b) on giving value for recommendations; the AICPA Commissions and Referral Fees Rule, ET 1.520.001) also favor "partner receives nothing" [verify current text]. TCPA: partner contacts are relationship-only, and texts to partners are file-service only through the gate. No rates in the room except a priced result with APR and the not-a-commitment line. Data is never instructions: partner-uploaded documents are parsed as data only.
- **Moat:** The verified-professional registry compounds: a CPA verified once for one borrower is recognised on the next file under any MLO, so partners face less friction the more MLOs use the platform. Because the room belongs to the borrower and the seat follows the MLO across sponsors, partner relationships aren't lost when an LO moves (active-loan copies stay with the sponsor per record-keeping rules). An institution-owned CRM can't credibly promise that.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: The consumer-direction exception (Reg P 1016.15(a)(1)) fits field-scoped grants the consumer can revoke. File status for a mutual client is service to the borrower, not a thing of value to the agent. Residual risks: (a) the anonymous scenario front door, where partners get free answers with no borrower file, is lawful only as 'normal promotional and educational activity' that isn't conditioned on referrals and doesn't defray costs the partner would otherwise incur (Reg X 1024.14(g)(1)(vi), from training knowledge); (b) automated license lookups against state commissions or NASBA CPAverify may breach those sites' terms of use. Redesign: Cap anonymous scenario answers at template text, with no worksheets or documents produced for a partner without a borrower file, and make the volume the same for every partner. Do license verification by hand, or through an API whose terms allow it, and record the check rather than scraping. / keep 6/10: Floify and Encompass agent portals push status to agents the lender chooses. Borrower-controlled, scoped and revocable grants are common in fintech (Plaid-style permissions) but not in broker CRMs. The non-agent roles (CPA, advisor, attorney) are speculative. Redesign: Make it the shared grant layer that Agent Line and Deal Clock use. Launch with agents only, and add the other roles as templates later. / keep 6/10: Agent status sharing is weekly-use and real. But agents already get status from LOS milestone emails, so the borrower-controlled log is a differentiator more than a time-saver. CPAs, advisors, attorneys and builders appear on a minority of files and won't learn a new tool for one client. The five role templates plus license lookups (state real estate commissions, CPAverify, state bars, each with different terms and no common API) is a lot of scope. The anonymous scenario front door adds a support queue for the MLO. Redesign: v1: the borrower invites any person to a field-scoped, expiring, revocable view, with one template for agents and one generic template, plus the 'who looked' log. Leave out automated license verification: record a license number the partner types in. Add role templates only when usage data shows demand.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md (sections 2.2, 2.3, 5.3), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (ideas 8, 11; Relay reciprocity reasoning), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md (D1 sign-in links and cc_audit; D2 relationship-only default; D3 gate), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1016/subpart-C/section-1016.15 (Reg P consent exception; not opened this session, known text), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-B/section-1024.14 (Reg X 1024.14; not opened this session), https://cpaverify.org (NASBA CPA license lookup; not opened this session), https://www.americanbar.org/groups/professional_responsibility/publications/model_rules_of_professional_conduct/rule_7_2_communications_concerning_a_lawyer_s_services_specific_rules/ (not opened this session), https://help.floify.com/en/articles/15141079-encompass-third-party-recipient-sync (via market survey)


## Offer Math Desk: seller credits, buydowns and buyer-agreement fees, checked against each program's limits (round 1, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**For a buyer's live file, shows the buyer and their agent how much seller or builder credit the loan program can actually use, how the buyer-broker fee in their written buyer agreement flows through financing, and whether a credit buys more as a rate buydown, closing costs or a price cut. Every rate comes from a dated priced result.**

- **Problem:** Since the NAR settlement practice changes (effective 2024-08-17), buyer-broker pay is negotiated in written buyer agreements and often asked of the seller as a concession. Agents now structure offers with seller credits, buyer-broker fees and buydowns without knowing the program's interested-party-contribution limits. In recalled agency rules: Fannie Mae caps IPCs at 3%, 6% or 9% by CLTV for primary homes and 2% for investment property; FHA at 6%; VA at 4% of reasonable value excluding normal closing costs and discount points; USDA at about 6%. A credit over the cap or over actual costs is wasted or forces a price change late in escrow. The repo already has temporary-buydown and points-breakeven calculators, but they don't apply program caps or the buyer's own priced options.
- **How it works:** Data: the buyer's program, LTV and price from the D6 LOS record. Offer terms the agent enters in the Partner Room: price, seller credit, the buyer-agreement fee and who pays it. A per-program IPC and fee-treatment rules table in config, with citations and counsel status. Priced results: because LoanM8 prices four fixed scenarios by hand (lib/pricing/daily.ts), the Desk creates a one-pass pricing request: no buydown, a 2-1 temporary buydown, and permanent points worth the credit. The LO runs it in ARIVE and enters it as a priced result for this file. Trigger: the agent taps "Check an offer" in the Partner Room, or the borrower asks M8. What the system does: computes the cap in dollars, usable credit (capped at actual closing costs and prepaids, with financed fees flagged), and how the buyer-broker fee is treated under the rules table. It compares cash to close, payment, 5-year cost and the payment the buyer qualifies at (a 2-1 buydown is qualified at the note rate). The output is a one-page sheet. What the MLO does: prices the three options and approves the sheet. What the agent experiences: "Up to $18,400 of seller credit is usable on this FHA file; above that it's wasted. On today's priced results the permanent buydown lowers the payment more over 5 years than the 2-1." The sheet makes no recommendation on what to offer. What the buyer experiences: the same sheet with APR and the not-a-commitment line.
- **Technologies:** Per-program IPC and buyer-broker-fee rules table (versioned config with citations), lib/pricing build, apr and canonical hashing, Existing TemporaryBuydownCalc and PointsBreakevenCalc logic, D6 LOS loan facts, Partner Room grants, On-demand pricing request queue for the LO (ARIVE manual pull), lib/m8/guard.ts on every generated sentence, PDF sheet generation
- **Why it's new:** Consumer buydown calculators (including LoanM8's own /calculators/temporary-buydown) and lender total-cost tools such as Mortgage Coach's TCA illustrate rates. As far as this research found, none applies the loan program's interested-party caps and the agency treatment of seller-paid buyer-broker fees to a specific live file, or produces a sheet for the agent built on that file's priced options. Verification caveat: search was exhausted and selling-guide.fanniemae.com could not be reached, so the IPC percentages and the 2024 agency clarifications on seller-paid buyer-broker commissions (Fannie, Freddie, FHA, and VA Circular 26-24-14 on veterans paying buyer-broker fees, plus any extension) are recalled and must be checked before the rules table ships.
- **Compliance design:** No rate outside a priced result: the three options are priced for this file and dated, with APR and the not-a-commitment line, and the guard blocks any other number. Reg Z advertising: the sheet is a per-file disclosure to the buyer and their agent, never published, and if it ever became an ad the 1026.24 trigger-term rules would apply [COUNSEL]. RESPA 8: a service to the borrower on a live file, identical for any agent on it, with nothing of value tied to referrals. Anti-steering and LO pay: all three options always shown, ordered by the borrower's stated goal, and the LO's pay is identical across them (lib/pricing/compRule.ts is the existing rule). Unauthorized practice: the sheet states financing math, never offer strategy or contract terms. Fair lending: identical treatment for every file. GLBA: agent sees it only under the borrower's grant.
- **Moat:** The rules table (caps, fee treatment, buydown eligibility per program and per wholesale lender overlay) is shared infrastructure that every MLO's corrections improve. Agents who learn to send "check an offer" requests to platform LOs keep doing it, and a network of independent MLOs can keep lender-overlay rows current faster than any single LO.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: It is compliant as designed: per-file priced options with APR, all options shown, and identical LO pay. Fact risk: the treatment of seller-paid buyer-broker fees as interested-party contributions has to match current guides. From training knowledge, Fannie and Freddie clarified in 2024 that customary seller-paid buyer-broker commissions are not IPCs. VA Circular 26-24-14 and any extension of it are unverified. If the sheet is shown to an agent who reuses it in marketing, it becomes a 1026.24 advertisement. Redesign: Watermark the sheet 'for [borrower] file only; not for advertising', and expire it with the priced snapshot. Ship rules-table rows only after counsel and guide verification, each with an effective date. / drop 4/10: Buydown and seller-concession calculators for agents are common (lender-branded agent tools, Mortgage Coach). IPC limits are already checked in the LOS and AUS. Only the per-file sheet for the agent is new, and it adds load to the manual pricing pull. Redesign: Make it one sheet template inside Partner Room, built from the Ad Desk and Quote Ledger priced-result binding. Don't build it as a separate desk. / keep 6/10: IPC caps are few, stable and public (from training knowledge, not re-verified: Fannie 3/6/9% by LTV, 2% for investment property; FHA 6%; VA 4% concessions on top of normal costs), so the rules table is cheap. Agents in competitive offers would use it. The weak point is the per-file 'one-pass pricing request' for three options. With manual ARIVE pulls, that adds 10 to 20 minutes of LO work per offer check, which kills weekly use. Buyer-broker fee treatment after the 2024 agency and VA clarifications needs verifying before shipping. Redesign: v1 computes the usable-credit cap and cash-to-close math with no rates. Rate comparisons show only when a daily priced scenario matches the file, and otherwise say 'ask your LO to price this'. Per-file pricing becomes optional, triggered by the LO.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/lib/pricing/daily.ts (four manually priced scenarios), /home/user/RATEM8/components/calc/TemporaryBuydownCalc.tsx, /home/user/RATEM8/lib/pricing/compRule.ts, /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md (sections 2.3, 2.5, 5.2), https://selling-guide.fanniemae.com/sel/b3-4.1-02/interested-party-contributions-ipcs (not reachable this session; host must be allowed), https://www.hud.gov/program_offices/housing/sfh/handbook_4000-1 (FHA 6% seller concessions; not opened this session), https://www.benefits.va.gov/homeloans/ (VA 4% concessions and Circular 26-24-14; not opened this session), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1026/subpart-C/section-1026.24 (Reg Z advertising; not opened this session)


## Application Clock: a TRID and Reg B sentinel across every AI channel, with a voice walk-through of the borrower's own Loan Estimate (round 1, high impact, ~6 weeks)

**Once AI agents chat, text, call and take uploads, the six TRID application facts can pile up across channels without anyone noticing. Application Clock tracks each fact's source, stops M8 from collecting the last one unless the loan officer chooses to, and runs the Loan Estimate, intent-to-proceed and Reg B clocks once an application exists.**

- **Problem:** Under Reg Z 1026.2(a)(3)(ii), a TRID 'application' exists once six facts are on file: name, income, SSN, property address, estimated value and loan amount sought. From then the creditor, or a broker on its behalf, has 3 business days to deliver a Loan Estimate (1026.19(e)(1)(iii), with broker delivery under (e)(1)(ii)). No fee except the credit-report fee may be charged before the borrower signals intent to proceed (1026.19(e)(2)(i)). Reg B 1002.9 separately requires notice of action taken, or a notice of incompleteness, within 30 days. Once M8 chat, M8 voice, texting (D4), uploads (Second Look) and the ARIVE sync all collect borrower facts, nobody can say when the six-fact threshold was crossed or by which channel. That turns an AI agent into a source of accidental TRID applications and missed Reg B clocks. Today's CRMs leave these clocks to the LOS, which only knows what someone typed into it. (Regulation text from model knowledge; ecfr.gov was blocked in this session.)
- **How it works:** DATA: a per-contact ledger `cc_application_elements`, with one row per element, the channel that supplied it (chat, voice transcript, text, upload, LOS sync, the loan officer by hand), a timestamp and a pointer to the evidence. SSN and DOB are still redacted by lib/m8/redact.ts and never stored. The ledger records only that the borrower offered an SSN in a channel that discards it, or that the LOS reports one on file. Whether a discarded SSN counts as 'received' is flagged for counsel.

TRIGGERS: every inbound message, call transcript, upload parse and ARIVE webhook or Zapier event (application date, key dates) updates the ledger.

WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES:
(1) A guard rule in the M8 tool layer: with five of six elements present, M8 may not ask for the sixth unless the loan officer has set 'application mode' for that contact. If the borrower volunteers it, M8 acknowledges it without asking follow-ups and alerts the loan officer.
(2) When all six exist, or the LOS shows an application date, it starts dated clocks. They use a business-day calendar (federal holidays, the general definition for LE delivery and the specific definition for waiting periods): LE due, the 7-business-day pre-consummation wait, the Closing Disclosure 3-day receipt, and Reg B day 30.
(3) The clocks feed the doc-chase agents. When a chase stalls near Reg B day 25, it drafts a notice of incompleteness under 1002.9(c)(2) for the loan officer or the lender's closer to approve. It never sends adverse-action notices itself.
(4) LE walk-through by voice: once the LOS reports an LE issued and the borrower books a call or calls in, M8 gives the AI disclosure and the recording notice, checks the property state against the loan officer's licenses, and walks through the borrower's own LE line by line using the Second Look decoder (lib/second-look/decode.ts). Every number M8 speaks must match the parsed LE, which is checked by the guard, and is introduced as 'your Loan Estimate dated X'. M8 never re-prices. At the end M8 asks whether the borrower intends to proceed. A yes is recorded on audio and in a transcript, then confirmed by a text or email the borrower taps. TRID commentary lets intent be expressed in any manner unless the creditor requires a specific one, so each lender's acceptance of AI-captured intent sits in a per-lender table [COUNSEL].

WHAT THE LOAN OFFICER SEES: on Today, 'Dana: 5 of 6 application facts on file (income from voice 11/12, value from chat 11/14). One more makes this an application; the LE would be due Friday.' On every file, a clock strip.

WHAT THE BORROWER EXPERIENCES: they never get asked for an SSN by an AI, they get their LE explained in plain language on their own schedule, and they confirm intent with one tap.
- **Technologies:** Neon Postgres ledger with row-level security, zod-validated AI extraction from transcripts and messages, lib/m8/redact.ts and guard.ts (new tool-level rule), Twilio ConversationRelay (cascaded speech-to-text, model, text-to-speech, so the guard checks text before it is spoken), Second Look LE parser and decoder, ARIVE Zapier triggers (key dates, loan trackers), business-day calendar service, Upstash QStash for durable clocks, E-SIGN confirmation link for intent to proceed
- **Why it's new:** LOS products compute TRID dates from the application date a person types in. They don't watch the conversational channels where AI now collects facts. POS products (Blend, nCino) open applications through structured forms, which is a different problem. The AI-voice vendors in the market survey (Sela, Flair, Shape, Total Expert's AI Sales Assistant) are built for lead conversion, and none of them publicly describes tracking TRID or Reg B triggers across channels (vendor marketing; unverified beyond the survey). ARIVE switched off its AI document beta on 2026-08-30 (survey fact), so broker stacks have no AI layer here at all. What is new is treating 'when did this become an application' as an output of the agent system rather than an LOS field, plus a guard that keeps the AI from creating one by accident. This session couldn't search further: the shared web-search budget was used up and ecfr.gov was blocked.
- **Compliance design:** TRID: never pushes a file across the threshold silently. Clocks are conservative, and when sources conflict the earliest plausible application date wins. Reg B and ECOA: drafts incompleteness notices for a human to approve, never auto-declines, and asks no questions about protected traits or alimony or public-assistance income unless the borrower relies on it (1002.5(d)(2)). The AI never states that an application is approved. AI disclosure and the recording notice come first on every call. The LE walk-through is a service call, booked or placed by the borrower. It carries no marketing content, so no new consent tier is needed, and the guard rejects any rate not on the parsed LE. GLBA: the evidence pointers are audited reads, and SSN and DOB are never stored by Command Center. Licensing: walk-throughs only in the loan officer's licensed states. Broker versus creditor duties and per-lender acceptance of AI-captured intent are marked [COUNSEL].
- **Moat:** The counsel-reviewed edge cases (what counts as an estimated value, a volunteered SSN, multi-borrower files) and the per-lender acceptance table grow with every member file and every sponsor. A solo loan officer can't build that rules library alone, and an MLO-owned network shares it without sharing any borrower data.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: It is sound overall. One real hole: treating a volunteered SSN that the redactor discards as possibly 'not received'. TRID turns on receiving the six items (1026.2(a)(3)(ii)). A regulator could view discarding as evading the trigger, so the conservative reading is that it was received. Intent to proceed may be given in any manner unless the creditor requires a specific one, and silence never counts (comment 19(e)(2)(i)(A)-2, from training knowledge), so the per-lender table is right. The business-day definitions are correctly assigned. The Application Meter in Licensed Hands duplicates this. Redesign: Treat a volunteered SSN in any channel as received for clock purposes. Start the clocks conservatively and alert the LO, even though the digits are discarded. Merge with Licensed Hands' Application Meter so there is one ledger. / keep 7/10: LOS compliance engines compute TRID dates only from an application date someone types in. No known AI-voice vendor counts the six application elements across channels to stop M8 creating an application by accident. It overlaps Licensed Hands' Application Meter. Redesign: Merge the element meter into Licensed Hands. Keep the TRID and Reg B clocks and the LE voice walk-through here, and feed the clocks into Deal Clock. / drop 5/10: The six-element meter overlaps 'Licensed Hands' (its Application Meter). Because M8 already redacts SSNs, the accidental-application risk is narrow. The LE, CD and waiting-period clocks duplicate what the LOS or creditor disclosure desk computes (ARIVE and Encompass track disclosure dates), and the creditor owns them. The voice LE walk-through is valuable but is a separate, liability-heavy build: number-matching guard and per-lender intent-to-proceed acceptance. Redesign: Merge the cross-channel element ledger into Licensed Hands. Read LE and CD dates from the LOS rather than computing them. Treat the LE walk-through as a separate later patch, chat first, with voice after the guard proves itself.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md (sections 2.1, 2.5, 4.6), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D2–D4 (consent log, redaction before send, send gate), /home/user/RATEM8/lib/second-look/decode.ts and lib/m8/guard.ts (existing parser and guard), https://support.arive.com/support/solutions/articles/61000319289-arive-ai-beta-doc-classification-extraction-update (ARIVE AI beta turned off, from the market survey), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1026/subpart-A/section-1026.2 (not opened: host blocked in this session), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1026/subpart-E/section-1026.19 (not opened: host blocked), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1002/section-1002.9 (not opened: host blocked)


## Third-Party Chase Agent: an AI that waits on hold with HOAs, insurance agents and payoff desks so the file doesn't wait (round 1, high impact, ~7 weeks)

**With the borrower's signed authorization, an AI agent emails and then calls the businesses a file depends on. It works through their phone menus, waits on hold, asks for exactly one document with a file reference code, checks the document when it arrives, and hands exceptions to a person.**

- **Problem:** For a broker's file, the slowest steps are rarely the borrower. They're third parties: condo and HOA questionnaires, the hazard-insurance binder with the correct mortgagee clause and replacement-cost estimate, payoff statements, verification of rent from property managers, CPA letters, and the HR contact needed for a verbal employment check. A solo loan officer or processor spends hours on hold and re-sending emails. The AI processors in the market survey (Friday Harbor, Candor, Vesta) work on the lender side, parse documents already received, or replace the LOS. None of them chases third parties for a broker.
- **How it works:** DATA: the open conditions on the file (ARIVE via Zapier or webhooks, or entered by hand), the borrower's signed third-party authorization captured at application (an e-signature, stored as proof), and a counterparty directory. Every phone number and email in the directory is sourced independently of the borrower (the company's own site, a state registry or a prior verified contact) and pinned at file open. A number taken from a borrower-supplied document is never used for verification, which is a fraud control.

TRIGGER: a condition is open longer than its expected turnaround (from the directory's history), or the loan officer taps 'Chase it'.

WHAT THE SYSTEM DOES:
(1) Email first, from the loan officer's domain: one specific ask, a secure upload link and a file reference code, and the minimum borrower data (the property address and the borrower's name for the HOA; no SSN, DOB or account numbers).
(2) If there's no reply in N business days, it calls. Twilio Lookup must classify the number as a landline or business line. If the line is wireless, it doesn't call unless that business contact has given consent, for example by replying to the email.
(3) On the call, it opens with: 'This is an automated assistant calling for [loan officer], NMLS [number], about a request authorized by your customer at [address]. This call is recorded.' It navigates phone menus by keypad tones, detects hold and waits, then asks for the document to be emailed or uploaded with the reference code. It answers only fixed questions (who it is, what is requested, who authorized it, how to reach a person). A request to stop calling goes into a business opt-out list, and 'I need to speak to a person' books or transfers to the loan officer or the exception desk.
(4) When a document arrives, it is checked: type, dates, names, and the mortgagee clause against the lender's clause from config. The card says 'received, looks complete' or 'received, missing replacement cost'. It never clears a condition in the LOS (write-back is notes only), and anything the counterparty says or sends is data, never instructions.
(5) Verbal employment checks: by default it only prepares the step (finds the HR contact or The Work Number route), because investors require the lender to document the person who made the verification. AI-made employment checks are allowed only for lenders that accept them in writing, set in a per-lender table.

WHAT THE LOAN OFFICER SEES: 'Insurance binder: called Smith Agency 2:10 PM, on hold 22 min, binder emailed 3:04, mortgagee clause correct.'

WHAT THE COUNTERPARTY EXPERIENCES: an honest, disclosed, short call with one ask.

WHAT THE BORROWER SEES: their file moving, with no extra asks.
- **Technologies:** Twilio Programmable Voice with answering-machine and hold detection, keypad tones, and ConversationRelay, Twilio Lookup for line type, Resend for outbound email, plus an inbound email intake address per file (vendor TBD), vision-model document checks with zod schemas, ARIVE Zapier and webhooks for conditions, Neon counterparty directory (business facts only), Upstash QStash for retries and turnaround timers, lib/m8/guard.ts and redact.ts on every utterance
- **Why it's new:** Mortgage AI processing tools in the survey (Friday Harbor, Addy AI, Candor DI, Loandock, Vesta) are lender-side document and underwriting tools, and ARIVE shut down its own document-AI beta on 2026-08-30. Healthcare has AI agents that call insurers to verify benefits (for example Infinitus), and Google has demonstrated AI that calls local businesses on a user's behalf. Both are recalled from model knowledge and couldn't be verified here, because the session's search budget was exhausted. I found nothing in this research that makes disclosed, authorized business-to-business chase calls for broker files. Pinning independently sourced counterparty contacts at file open also guards against forged verifications.
- **Compliance design:** TCPA: 47 U.S.C. 227(b)(1)(A)(iii) requires prior express consent for any artificial-voice call to a wireless number, whatever the purpose. So the agent calls only numbers classified as landline or business lines, or a business contact who consented. The calls are informational and not telemarketing, but the internal business opt-out list is honored immediately. AI disclosure comes at the start of every call (California Bus. & Prof. Code 17941 and state bot laws), and the recording notice is given on every call, not only in all-party states, which is simpler and safe. GLBA: disclosures are limited to what's needed to complete the transaction the consumer requested (the GLBA 502(e) exception, [COUNSEL]), every read is audited, and no SSN, DOB or account numbers are spoken or stored. Wiretap risk (CIPA, Ambriz v. Google): the voice vendor is under zero-retention, no-training terms. RESPA: no payments to counterparties, and questionnaire fees are passed through as invoiced. No rates or loan terms are ever discussed. Investor rules on who may perform a verbal employment check are a hard per-lender gate.
- **Moat:** The counterparty directory gets better with every member's calls: which payoff desks answer by email, the phone-menu path to a person at each HOA manager or insurance agency, and typical turnaround. It holds business facts only, never borrower data. Ten thousand files' worth of menu maps and response patterns is something no single broker can build, and only a network that shares it can keep it current. [COUNSEL: confirm that sharing third-party vendor fee schedules among members raises no antitrust issue.]
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: Calls to wireless lines are handled. The gap is residential landlines. Twilio Lookup cannot tell a home-based HOA manager or a sole-proprietor agent's residential landline from a business line. Artificial-voice calls to residential lines without consent are allowed only under the 47 CFR 64.1200(a)(3) exemptions, which since the 2020 TRACED Act order (from training knowledge) cap informational calls at 3 in any 30 days and require an automated opt-out mechanism (64.1200(b)(2)-(3)). California B&P 17941 covers online bots, so it may not reach voice calls, though disclosing anyway is fine. GLBA disclosure to effect the consumer's requested transaction (6802(e)(1)) fits. Redesign: Treat every non-wireless number as potentially residential. Apply the 3-calls-per-30-days cap and a press-key or spoken automated opt-out to all chase calls. Prefer email and the counterparty's published business line from the directory, and stop calling once any reply comes back. / keep 6/10: Healthcare has the same pattern (Infinitus-style benefit verification calls). Truv and The Work Number already automate VOE digitally, and HomeWiseDocs and CondoCerts automate HOA documents, so part of the gap is already filled. I know of no broker-side tool that chases with disclosed, authorized calls, but this is unverified. Redesign: Go email and portal first, routing HOA requests to HomeWiseDocs or CondoCerts and VOE to existing services. Use calls only for insurance binders and payoffs, where no portal exists. / keep 4/10: Phone chasing buys less than it seems. HOA and condo documents are mostly ordered through paid management-company portals (HomeWiseDocs, CondoCerts and similar; recalled, not verified). Payoffs come from servicer portals or title. Insurance binders usually arrive by email. Building AI IVR navigation, hold detection and fixed-question dialogue is a big build with recurring per-minute voice cost, landline classification and a business opt-out list. The verbal VOE piece is gated by investors anyway. The email-first chase with a secure upload link and a pinned directory is the cheap 80%.
