Under the trust, these reserved matters need a member referendum: (1) sale, merger or change of control; (2) any change to the data charter (no lead sale, no aggregate data sale, no reassigning a departed MLO's relationship contacts, no consumer-report ingestion); (3) seat-price increases above a published index cap (for example, CPI plus pass-through vendor cost changes, itemized); (4) new unit issuance above an annual dilution cap; (5) removal of the comp firewall or of any section 2 rule. In the product, a /mlo/governance page shows the covenants, the current price-cap math, every reserved-matter proposal, and a secure ballot with eligibility checked against the ledger. A sale proposal triggers an automatic 60-day member right of first offer to buy the company through a member vehicle (an exit-to-community conversion). The results and the trust protector's annual report are published. Outside investors get economic units and ordinary board seats, but not the reserved matters, and that is disclosed up front.
- **Technologies:** Delaware PBC (DGCL 361–368) or a purpose-clause LLC operating agreement, Non-charitable purpose trust with an elected trust protector, Ballot service with ledger-checked eligibility and signed results (KMS), Published price-cap calculator fed by itemized vendor-cost pass-throughs, Covenant registry versioned like the D3 rules table (effective-dated, with citations), Board and cap-table records in the D12 ledger
- **Why it's new:** From general knowledge, not re-verified this session: purpose-trust stewardship has precedents outside mortgage. Patagonia (2022) put all voting stock in the Patagonia Purpose Trust, and Organically Grown Company (2018) uses a perpetual purpose trust. Exit to Community (Zebras Unite and the Media Enterprise Design Lab, 2020) describes converting startups to user ownership. In the survey, no mortgage CRM, LOS or AI-voice vendor has member-held vetoes. Lenders One is managed by Altisource, CUSOs are owned by credit unions, and eXp's and Real's agent stockholders have no reserved-matter votes. The survey's data-charter idea covers data only. This idea also locks sale, price and dilution, and adds a member buyout right when a sale is proposed.
- **Compliance design:** [COUNSEL: corporate and securities] Purpose-trust validity and duration under the chosen state's statute. Voting by unit holders may affect the securities analysis and the 12(g) count. Proxy and ballot disclosures. Antifraud: the covenants must be described accurately, including what outside investors can still do. [COUNSEL: mortgage] Governance rights are not compensation. Votes are one member one vote, never weighted by production or loans, so nothing relates to loan terms (Reg Z 1026.36(d)). Agents and other referral sources can't vote or hold units (RESPA 8). The data charter stays inside GLBA: borrower NPI belongs to the sponsor, and the charter limits only what the platform may do with it. Antitrust: members vote on platform governance, never on their own pricing to borrowers, comp levels or territories. The ballot service blocks agenda items of that kind, and counsel reviews each one. Price-cap copy is policy language, never 'lowest price' or urgency.
- **Moat:** This is structural: a competitor owned by a data vendor, an LOS vendor or a PE fund can't adopt it without its owners giving up control. Each year the covenants hold builds trust with recruits that no one can buy quickly. The more members there are, the more legitimate the vote and the higher the cost to any acquirer of trying to get around it.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: No mortgage-law violation: governance votes are one member one vote, unrelated to loans. Risks are corporate and securities: purpose-trust validity and duration (Delaware 12 Del. C. 3556 and Oregon permit them, from training knowledge), voting rights affecting the securities analysis, and full disclosure to outside investors. Members voting on seat prices are buyers voting on the JV's own price, which is low antitrust risk if it is limited to caps and never touches members' prices to borrowers. Redesign: Keep the ballot-agenda block on member pricing, comp and territories. Have counsel opine on the reserved matters' effect on 12(g) and exemption choice before issuance. / keep 7/10: Precedents exist outside mortgage (Patagonia Purpose Trust, Organically Grown Company, Exit to Community), but I know of none in mortgage tech. It is the most credible proof of the 'MLO-owned' promise. Redesign: Write the trust protector and reserved matters into the founding documents now, since they are hard to add later. / keep 4/10: A purpose trust holding golden shares is a high legal setup cost and can deter outside investors at seed stage. A referendum system for reserved matters is more build. The underlying promise (no sale of data, price caps, dilution caps, member vote on sale) is valuable for recruiting and can mostly be had more cheaply. Redesign: Now: put member protective provisions in the LLC operating agreement (data charter, price-increase cap formula, dilution cap, member consent on change of control) and a simple ballot page. Revisit a purpose trust at a priced round or before any sale process.
- **Sources:** https://www.housingwire.com/articles/mmi-acquires-crm-platform-bonzo/ (Bonzo sold to MMI; survey), https://www.icemortgagetechnology.com/products/surefire-mortgage-crm (Surefire owned by ICE; survey), https://www.rismedia.com/2026/07/30/lpt-realty-takes-official-step-to-go-public/ (agent-stake platforms going public; survey), https://stocks.observer-reporter.com/observerreporter/article/gnwcq-2026-3-2-lenders-one-unites-members-and-providers-at-2026-summit-welcomes-new-president (Lenders One managed by Altisource; survey), https://www.patagonia.com/ownership/ (Patagonia Purpose Trust; canonical URL, NOT opened this session), https://delcode.delaware.gov/title12/c035/sc04/index.html (12 Del. C. 3556 purpose trusts; fetch failed DNS this session), https://delcode.delaware.gov/title8/c001/sc15/ (DGCL public benefit corporations; NOT opened)


## Gate Warranty and Member Defense Reserve (round 1, high impact, ~5 weeks)

**If the send gate said 'allowed' and the facts it recorded show it was wrong, the platform pays to defend the MLO and covers the loss up to a published cap, from a reserve the members govern. Compliance becomes a guarantee the vendor stands behind, not a disclaimer.**

- **Problem:** A solo or small-broker MLO who leaves a large company loses its legal department. TCPA statutory damages are $500 per call or text, trebled if willful (47 U.S.C. 227(b)(3)). Mortgage TCPA filings surged in 2026 (NMN counts 11 in about two months, then nine more; Loanstream faces allegations over 272,092 calls). Many general liability and E&O policies exclude TCPA claims. Every CRM and AI-voice vendor sells 'compliance features' under terms that disclaim liability, so the MLO bears all the risk of the vendor's logic being wrong. That fear keeps MLOs at large lenders.
- **How it works:** Data: every D3 decide() result is already logged with its inputs, reasons, consent basis and rule version. Covered event: the MLO sent through the gate, without overriding or editing a blocked decision, and the gate's logged decision was wrong under the rules in force that day given the facts it recorded. Examples: a quiet-hours miscalculation, a state cap bug, an opt-out the system received but didn't apply. Not covered: facts the MLO attested falsely (such as a fabricated consent), manual dials outside the gate, and anything sent while on hold. When a demand letter or complaint arrives, the MLO uploads it. The system pulls the contact's full gate history, consent proofs, opt-out list state and rule versions into a counsel-ready packet (it reuses the evidence-packet work), and a coverage engine answers in plain language whether the warranty applies and why. A panel defense firm, retained by the platform, takes covered matters. The reserve is funded by a fixed line in each seat price, shown on the invoice, plus a tech E&O and cyber policy naming members as additional insureds where the carrier allows. Members see the reserve balance, matters (anonymized) and payouts quarterly, and the reserve's level is a member-governed reserved matter. Any bug a claim reveals triggers a network-wide rule fix and a re-scan of all tenants for the same pattern before the next send window.
- **Technologies:** D3 send-gate decision log with rule-version stamping, Evidence packet builder (consent proofs, Twilio delivery receipts, cc_optouts state, DNC and Reassigned Numbers Database checks), Coverage-determination rules engine (pure function, tested like decide()), Network-wide regression scan job across tenants after any covered defect, Tech E&O and cyber insurance with additional-insured endorsements, Reserve ledger (segregated account) with a quarterly member report
- **Why it's new:** In the survey, none of the CRMs or AI-voice vendors (Bonzo, Shape, Total Expert, Sela, Flair, Structurely) publishes a warranty on its own compliance logic. Several sell exactly the high-volume dialing model the 2026 suits target. Consent and DNC vendors (Gryphon, PossibleNOW, DNC.com) sell scrubs, not a defense commitment tied to a per-decision audit trail. The survey's 'TCPA demand-letter responder' produces evidence. This idea adds the financial promise and a member-governed reserve, which an MLO-owned platform can credibly offer because its members are its owners and decide how the risk is pooled.
- **Compliance design:** [COUNSEL: insurance regulation] It must stay a warranty of the platform's own service, not insurance against the MLO's own conduct. That means covering only defects in the platform's decisions, capping it, and attaching it to the subscription, which is the usual line between a product warranty and insurance. Counsel confirms per state, and if a state treats it as insurance, it is restructured through a licensed carrier or a risk retention group. [COUNSEL: TCPA] Coverage is never a reason to loosen the gate. The gate stays at the strictest reading, and the warranty only covers its errors. Reg Z: the reserve line is flat per seat, never per loan or per send, and payouts track defects, not loan activity, so nothing relates to loan terms. RESPA 8: no agent or referral source is a beneficiary. GLBA: packets hold borrower NPI, so they go only to the panel firm under a service-provider agreement, with read-audit entries. check:copy: no 'guaranteed compliance'. The wording is 'what we cover and what we don't', with the cap stated.
- **Moat:** The warranty is only affordable if the gate is right, so every defect fixed after a claim hardens the gate for all members. Loss data across many tenants lets the platform price the reserve better than any single MLO or competitor could. A vendor that disclaims liability today would have to rebuild its logging to the per-decision standard D3 already meets before it could offer the same.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: A capped warranty limited to the platform's own defects is ordinarily contractual indemnity, not insurance, if it is narrowly drafted. One issue is overlooked. Panel defense counsel retained and paid by the platform to defend the MLO, where the platform's own defect is at issue, raises ABA Model Rules 1.7, 1.8(f) and 5.4(c) conflicts. Counsel must take no direction from the payer and needs the client's informed consent. The platform's interests can diverge, for example in blaming the MLO's attestation. Redesign: Let the MLO choose from the panel or bring their own counsel, with the platform reimbursing up to the cap. Sign an engagement letter disclaiming platform control. Have an independent reviewer, not the platform, make coverage decisions on disputed 'attested falsely' exclusions. / keep 6/10: Accuracy guarantees are common in adjacent fields (TurboTax, payroll tax-filing guarantees). I know of no TCPA or consent vendor that warrants its per-decision logic, which is plausible but unverified. The insurance-regulation risk is real. Redesign: Pair it with Proof-Priced Coverage: a capped service warranty backed by tech E&O, with the reserve as a later reserved matter. / drop 2/10: The economics don't hold. TCPA damages are $500 per violation, trebled to $1,500 when willful (47 U.S.C. 227(b)(3)), and suits are class actions. One gate bug repeats across every tenant and send at once, so losses are correlated and can be very large, and a reserve funded by a flat per-seat line would be insolvent after one event. It also risks being treated as unlicensed insurance in some states. Defense-panel retainers are an ongoing cost. Redesign: Drop the indemnity. Offer instead: a published SLA with subscription credits for a confirmed gate defect, an automatic evidence packet for any demand letter, a network-wide rescan and disclosure after any bug, and a tech E&O policy for the platform's own liability.
- **Sources:** https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/mortgage-tcpa-lawsuits-surge-with-11-new-cases (survey), https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/tcpa-lawsuits-hit-nine-more-mortgage-lenders (survey), https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/tcpa-class-action-targets-loanstream-over-272k-spam-calls (survey), https://tcpaworld.com/2026/06/04/april-tcpa-filings-off-the-chart-330-tcpa-cases-255-tcpa-class-actions-filed-in-april-2026-up-40-from-2025/ (survey), https://www.housingwire.com/articles/west-capital-flair-ai-voice/ (high-volume dialing model; survey), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-64/subpart-L/section-64.1200 (TCPA rules; NOT opened this session), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D3 and D4 (decide(), decision log, cc_optouts)


## Sponsor Commons with a Portable Seat Addendum (round 1, high impact, ~6 weeks)

**A network of sponsoring brokerages that each sign one standard addendum: the MLO's relationship contacts and platform seat stay the MLO's, the sponsor keeps its loan files, and the sponsor's comp plan passes a Reg Z lint. A loan officer leaving a large company can choose a license home on known terms, and the platform takes no placement fee.**

- **Problem:** To leave a large lender, a loan officer needs a new sponsoring company, and today that is an opaque search through recruiters. Modex, for one, charges employers 10 basis points on the hire's 12-month production. Each new sponsor's contract re-litigates who owns the database (NEXA had to change its contracts after recruits objected), and small brokers often run comp plans that drift into Reg Z problems: pay varying by product, or by rate. The survey's 'seat follows the MLO' idea needs sponsors to agree contractually, or it is just a feature the old sponsor can override.
- **How it works:** Sponsors (broker companies with an NMLS company license in the relevant states) join the Commons by signing the Portable Seat Addendum, a counsel-drafted standard that maps to D2 provenance tags. Personal and relationship-only contacts follow the MLO. Active-loan and record-keeping files stay with the sponsor (copies kept for its retention periods). Neither side may reassign the other's relationship contacts. Each sponsor builds a private offer card: states, LOS and PPE used, the lender panel, fee structure, and its comp-plan type. Before the card can be shown, its comp plan runs through a Reg Z linter. It must not vary by rate, product type or other term or proxy, it uses the 10% non-deferred profits test calculator, it applies the designated-plan exception correctly, and it keeps records for 3 years under 1026.25(c)(2). A loan officer in Command Center who opens 'Find a sponsor' filters by their licensed states and sees only the cards that match. Sponsors never see each other's cards or comp figures, and the system aggregates none. The loan officer requests intros by hand. A sponsor can make an offer, and the MLO's existing seat, book and plays move under the new sponsor tenant in one step when the NMLS sponsorship relationship is confirmed. The platform charges sponsors only their flat seat subscriptions, never per hire or per basis point.
- **Technologies:** Standard contract addendum with clause IDs mapped to D2 provenance tags, Reg Z comp-plan linter (structured plan schema plus rule checks, 1026.36(d) and 1026.25(c)(2)), Tenant re-parenting in Neon with row-level security (seat and book move, sponsor files copied and retained), Private offer cards with per-viewer access control (no cross-sponsor visibility), NMLS sponsorship confirmation by the sponsor's attestation and an uploaded NMLS record (no bulk Consumer Access monitoring), Audit log of every intro and offer
- **Why it's new:** In the survey, recruiting economics in this market are production-linked: Modex's 10-basis-point placement fee, NEXA's 10 bps per recruit loan, One Real's RSU once a recruit closes. Database ownership is settled one contract at a time (NMN's 'only the contract knows'; NEXA's revised contracts). No platform offers a multi-sponsor network with one portability standard and an automated Reg Z check on the comp plans sponsors offer. The survey's seat-portability and sponsor-handoff ideas are the technical mechanism. This is the contractual network that makes them enforceable, and the recruiting surface that brings in MLOs from large companies.
- **Compliance design:** [COUNSEL: antitrust] The DOJ/FTC worker-related guidance (January 2025, status under the current administration not verified this session) treats sharing wage and comp information among competing employers as risky. So offer cards are one-to-one, from one sponsor to one candidate, sponsors never see one another's terms, and the platform publishes no comp benchmarks. [COUNSEL: mortgage] The linter is a tool, not legal advice: the sponsor's counsel signs the plan. The platform takes no per-hire or production-based fee. State licensing: the platform is not the employer and does not pay MLOs, and the MLO's sponsor relationship is set in NMLS by the sponsor. NMLS Consumer Access terms bar solicitation and bulk monitoring, so no outreach to MLOs is sourced from NMLS. MLOs come in through their own sign-up. RESPA 8 is not implicated by sponsor-to-MLO employment terms, but agents are excluded from the network. GLBA: when a seat moves, borrower NPI on active loans stays with the old sponsor unless the borrower opts in. Non-solicits: the D2 non-solicit locks carry over unchanged. check:copy: no scarcity, earnings claims or 'commission'.
- **Moat:** Each sponsor that signs the addendum makes the network more valuable to every MLO, and each MLO who moves on standard terms makes signing it more attractive to the next sponsor, a two-sided network. Once the addendum is the norm, large companies whose contracts claim the LO's database look worse by comparison. The linter's rule set also improves with every plan it reviews.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: (1) GLBA: 'relationship-only contacts follow the MLO' moves sponsor customers' NPI to a new nonaffiliated sponsor's tenant. That needs privacy-notice disclosure and an opt-out (Reg P 1016.10), and opt-in in California and Vermont. Personal, non-customer contacts are fine. (2) Antitrust: the January 2025 DOJ/FTC worker guidelines' current status is unverified. A platform that holds every sponsor's comp-plan structure through the linter is a potential hub for information exchange, so the linter's data must never inform other sponsors or any benchmarks. (3) NMLS Consumer Access sourcing is avoided, which is good. Redesign: Have the addendum carry only personal-provenance contacts plus sponsor customers with a recorded no-opt-out, or a CA and VT opt-in. Process comp plans in the linter in the sponsor's tenant with no cross-tenant analytics, and delete them after the lint result. / keep 7/10: Recruiting networks (Modex, NEXA, Real) are paid by production. I know of no multi-sponsor portability standard with Reg Z comp-plan linting. It needs sponsors to sign. Redesign: Sign it as one package with the Mobility Protocol. / keep 6/10: A strong recruiting story and a cheap build (offer cards and filters). The addendum is a one-time legal drafting cost. Weak spots: the sponsor side is a cold-start two-sided network, the Reg Z comp-plan linter can only check what a sponsor enters (it can't certify), and offer-card upkeep falls on busy small brokers. It absorbs the Mobility Protocol cleanly. Redesign: Ship the standard addendum (including the Mobility Protocol's contact-only portability and matching-list clauses) plus private one-to-one offer cards. Build the comp linter as a checklist with a 10% calculator, not an approval. Recruit 3 to 5 founding sponsors by hand before building the search surface.
- **Sources:** https://www.nationalmortgagenews.com/news/who-owns-client-data-only-the-contract-knows (survey), https://www.housingwire.com/articles/ccm-enforcement-action-non-solicitation/ (survey), https://nationalmortgageprofessional.com/news/nexa-removes-guardrails-its-100-revenue-model (NEXA 10 bps per recruit loan; survey), https://mortgageorb.com/one-real-mortgage-launches-compensation-and-stock-awards-programs-for-los (survey), https://mortgage.nationwidelicensingsystem.org/about/policies/NMLS%20Document%20Library/Consumer%20Access%20Terms%20of%20Use.pdf (no solicitation or bulk monitoring; survey), https://files.consumerfinance.gov/f/201301_cfpb_final-rule_loan-originator-compensation.pdf (10% rule and plan exceptions; survey), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/2026-04-08/interp-36/ (survey), https://www.wsgr.com/en/insights/doj-and-ftc-withdraw-antitrust-guidelines-for-competitor-collaborations.html (repo source on antitrust)


## Downturn Keep-Alive: pause the seat, freeze the clock (round 1, medium impact, ~3 weeks)

**When volume collapses, an MLO can drop to a low keep-alive seat that freezes, rather than forfeits, their vesting and tenure, keeps their book and consents intact, and puts outreach in service-only mode. MLOs who leave the industry in a bad year come back to their stake instead of starting over.**

- **Problem:** Mortgage runs in cycles. Blend guided Q4 2026 industry units down 28–33%, and in a downturn loan officers leave the industry or let licenses lapse. Every competing stake surveyed (One Real's production-gated RSUs, reAlpha's trailing-12-month production gate with continued employment, NEXA's per-loan streams) is lost exactly when an MLO can least afford to keep producing. A subscription-and-tenure plan has the same flaw if a cancelled subscription resets tenure: a subscription-only stake could punish the people it should keep.
- **How it works:** Data: billing state, the ownership ledger, and the MLO's license status as the MLO and sponsor attest it (with the NMLS record uploaded, never bulk-monitored). Trigger: the MLO chooses 'keep-alive' in billing, or the sponsor reports the license inactive or the sponsorship ended. What the system does: the seat drops to a flat keep-alive price that covers storage, the consent ledger and opt-out compliance. Vesting and the tenure clock freeze: paused months accrue zero platform-months and units, but nothing already vested or accrued is forfeited, and the cliff doesn't reset. The book becomes read-only for the MLO. The send gate blocks all marketing and every M8 outbound call, while inbound replies and STOPs are still processed and honored network-wide. Borrowers who message get an honest auto-reply naming the sponsor's active contact for in-flight loans, or saying 'not taking new loans right now' for relationship-only contacts. LoanM8 routing marks the MLO unavailable, so M8 never routes a borrower to an inactive license. A paused MLO can still vote on reserved matters. When the license is active again with a sponsor, one tap restores the seat, plays come back in a re-permission-first state (consents past their counsel-set freshness are re-checked), and the clock resumes. MLOs see 'Clock paused since [date]; nothing forfeited.'
- **Technologies:** Stripe subscription with a keep-alive plan and pause and resume events, Ownership ledger accrual rules (zero-accrual paused state, no forfeiture), D3 send gate 'service-only' posture flag, lib/routing.ts availability flag so M8 doesn't route to inactive MLOs, Inbound auto-reply via the Twilio inbound webhook, Consent freshness re-check job on resume (Reassigned Numbers Database, DNC)
- **Why it's new:** In the survey, every mortgage stake is production- or employment-gated (One Real, reAlpha, NEXA), and eXp's and Real's revenue share depend on transactions. I found no CRM that offers a licensing-aware hibernation mode that keeps consent and opt-out compliance running while the seat is paused. Vendors usually just cancel and delete, or keep billing (the Shape complaints). Freezing tenure-based vesting across a pause is a design only a subscription-and-tenure plan can offer, and it directly answers the 'what happens in a bad year' objection from large-company MLOs.
- **Compliance design:** Licensing: an inactive or unsponsored MLO must not originate. The gate blocks all loan marketing and M8 never routes borrowers to them (§2.1 licensing rule). License status comes from the MLO's and sponsor's attestation, not from NMLS Consumer Access scraping (its terms bar bulk monitoring). TCPA: opt-outs and revocations are still processed during the pause and apply network-wide (One Stop). Auto-replies are service-only and carry no marketing. Reg Z: accrual depends only on paid seat-months, so a pause accrues zero and there is no link to loans. [COUNSEL: securities] The plan documents define the pause as a vesting suspension, not a termination, and the effect on any Rule 144 holding period is unaffected (holding runs from acquisition). [COUNSEL: mortgage] Keep-alive pricing is a flat fee. GLBA: active-loan borrower NPI stays with the sponsor and the paused MLO's read-only access is logged. Record-keeping holds apply. check:copy: no urgency or 'don't lose your stake' scare copy, just the policy as written.
- **Moat:** Retention through the trough compounds: the MLOs who return when rates fall bring their re-permissioned books and their tenure, while competitors have to recruit from zero each upturn. Each cycle the platform survives with its paused members intact adds proof for recruits. Competitors whose stakes are production-based can't copy this without abandoning their model.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: It is compliant: no origination while inactive, routing off, opt-outs still honored. Small risks: the auto-reply to relationship contacts must not contain marketing or loan terms, or it would be licensed activity by an inactive licensee. A paused MLO's read-only access to sponsor-customer NPI after sponsorship ends may exceed what the old sponsor permits. Redesign: When sponsorship ends, remove access to sponsor-customer records, leaving only personal-provenance contacts readable. Keep auto-reply text fixed and counsel-approved. / keep 5/10: Pause plans are common in SaaS. Freezing tenure vesting rather than forfeiting it is new only within this platform's ownership plan. It is a plan term, not a product. Redesign: Write it into the D12 plan documents and billing as a seat state, rather than treating it as a separate idea. / keep 7/10: Cheap: a billing state, a vesting-pause flag, gate rules already in D3 and a routing 'unavailable' state. It directly lowers churn in rate-driven downturns, which is when subscription businesses in this market lose members. The keep-alive price has to cover fixed carrier and compliance costs (10DLC campaign fees, inbound STOP handling, storage), or it loses money per paused seat. Redesign: Build as specified. Set keep-alive pricing from measured fixed cost per seat. Decide with counsel whether a paused 10DLC campaign is kept registered or re-registered on return.
- **Sources:** https://finviz.com/news/418039/blend-labs-posts-its-best-sales-quarter-in-three-years-but-a-mortgage-downturn-pushes-q4-guidance-into-decline (industry units down 28–33%; survey), https://www.quiverquant.com/news/reAlpha+Tech+Corp+Launches+National+Loan+Officer+Recruitment+Program+with+RSU+Incentives (production gate and continued employment; survey), https://nationalmortgageprofessional.com/news/one-real-mortgage-introduces-loan-officer-incentives (survey), https://www.capterra.co.uk/reviews/173673/setshape (billing after cancellation; survey), https://mortgage.nationwidelicensingsystem.org/about/policies/NMLS%20Document%20Library/Consumer%20Access%20Terms%20of%20Use.pdf (no bulk monitoring; survey), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md idea 12 and 'One Stop, everywhere'


## Refund Radar: every dollar owed back to the borrower, on its legal clock (round 1, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**After a closing, payoff, PMI cancellation or insurance fix, Command Center opens an 'owed to you' item with its statutory deadline, tracks it until the borrower confirms receipt, and drafts the borrower's own Reg X letter if the deadline passes.**

- **Problem:** Money is routinely owed to homeowners after a mortgage event, each item has a federal deadline, and nobody on the borrower's side watches the clock. [HIGH] Reg X 1024.34(b): the old servicer must return the escrow balance within 20 days (excluding weekends and legal holidays) of a payoff. [HIGH] TRID 1026.19(f)(2)(v): tolerance cures are due within 60 days of consummation. [HIGH] Reg X 1024.17(f): an escrow surplus of $50 or more is refunded within 30 days of the analysis if the loan is current. [MED-HIGH] Homeowners Protection Act: unearned PMI premiums come back within 45 days of cancellation. [HIGH] Reg X 1024.37(g): within 15 days of evidence of coverage, the servicer cancels force-placed insurance and refunds the overlap. [MED] FHA's UFMIP refund credit applies when an FHA loan is refinanced into a new FHA loan within 3 years. [MED] VA refunds the funding fee when a veteran's disability rating is made effective on or before closing. Servicers run these clocks against themselves. A borrower who is never told is never paid, and a tolerance cure the MLO's own file missed is an exam finding.
- **How it works:** Data: the D6 POS sync (closing date, LE and CD fee lines, payoff date of the loan being refinanced), plus borrower uploads through the Verified Loan Facts path (CD, escrow analysis, PMI cancellation letter, refund checks), plus what the borrower tells M8 ('VA just rated me, effective last March'). Triggers: (1) A CD arrives before funding. Code, not AI, diffs the CD against the last LE by tolerance bucket (zero, 10% aggregate, unlimited). Any cure owed puts a card on the MLO's Today before funding and starts a 60-day clock, because a cure caught early is the cheapest one. (2) A refinance funds. A 20-business-day clock starts on the old servicer's escrow refund, with an estimate from the old statement's escrow balance and a federal-holiday calendar. (3) A PMI cancellation is confirmed (feeds from PMI Exit Concierge). A 45-day clock starts. (4) An escrow analysis upload shows a surplus. A 30-day clock starts. (5) Coverage Guard (below) sends evidence of coverage. A 15-day clock starts. (6) The borrower reports a VA rating, or an FHA streamline is in the file. A neutral explainer shows the refund or credit path. What the system does: a private 'Money owed to you' page for the borrower, with each item, its rule, the deadline and its status. One service-only reminder through the D3 send gate. When a clock passes without 'I got it', M8 drafts the borrower's own written request for information or notice of error (Reg X 1024.36 and 1024.35), addressed to the servicer's designated address, for the borrower to sign and send. Follow-ups come at the servicer's 5-business-day acknowledgment point and its 30-business-day response point. What the MLO does: approves the first message, works tolerance-cure cards with the lender, and gets a one-line Today card ('Dana's $1,412 escrow refund from her old servicer is 6 days late; letter drafted'). The borrower gets money back they didn't know about, from their own loan officer.
- **Technologies:** lib/second-look parser extended with LE-to-CD tolerance bucket diff (deterministic code), D6 POS connector (ARIVE webhooks or Zapier) for closing and payoff dates, QStash delayed jobs with a business-day and federal-holiday calendar, D3 send gate, service purpose only, PDF letter generation for borrower-signed Reg X 1024.35/1024.36 letters, Neon owed_items table, append-only, with row-level security, Resend and Twilio (D4) for service reminders, Servicer designated-address table (procedural facts only)
- **Why it's new:** Servicers send refunds and run their own clocks. LOS fee-tolerance checks exist inside lender compliance workflows. Consumer guides explain each rule separately. From the market survey (Bonzo, Shape, Total Expert, Homebot, BNTouch, Jungo), every past-client feature is a date drip, equity email or refi alert; none tracks money owed to the borrower against statutory deadlines. The repo's ideas doc covers PMI removal requests (idea 9) and escrow shortage forecasts (idea 10), not refunds, and the survey's Escrow Statement Checker audits the annual analysis only. Refund Radar adds payoff escrow, tolerance cures, HPA premium refunds, force-placed overlap refunds, and the VA and FHA credits on one clocked ledger. Caveat: this session's web search budget was exhausted and ecfr.gov was blocked, so 'no competitor does this' rests on the survey's vendor scans plus training knowledge, not a fresh search.
- **Compliance design:** Service messages only. No refinance or rate content, so no marketing consent is needed for email. Texts still need permission on file and pass the D3 gate (quiet hours, opt-out). No rate is ever stated, and dollar figures come only from code over the borrower's own documents, labelled 'estimate from your statement'. The letters are the borrower's own, signed and sent by the borrower. The platform never acts as their agent, charges nothing and gives no legal advice [COUNSEL: wording]. VA disability status is borrower-volunteered only. It is stored as 'funding fee exemption: claimed', is never a ranking or outreach input, and is excluded from every model, since disability is a Fair Housing Act protected class. The FHA UFMIP credit is mentioned only inside a file that is already an FHA refinance, or when the borrower asks, never as a pitch. HPPA: no credit data. GLBA: uploads deleted after extraction, every read audited. RESPA 8: no party pays or receives anything. Fair lending: every closing gets the same ledger.
- **Moat:** Two network effects that grow with members and time. (1) Servicers may designate an exclusive address for notices of error and information requests. Each borrower upload adds servicer-level procedural facts (designated address, refund-check format, insurance-processing address) to a shared table, with no borrower NPI extracted. After a few thousand members' closings it becomes the most complete servicer correspondence directory an originator has. (2) Every closing on the platform starts with its clocks already running, so a borrower who has been refunded once has a concrete reason to come back. Any aggregate servicer timeliness view waits for counsel review and a minimum count, given the antitrust caution in the ideas doc.
- **Reviews:** keep 9/10: The deadlines match training knowledge: tolerance cure within 60 days after consummation (1026.19(f)(2)(v)), payoff escrow refund within 20 days (1024.34(b)), HPA unearned premium within 45 days, escrow surplus within 30 days of analysis (1024.17(f)), and force-placed refund within 15 days (1024.37(g)). Service-only, borrower-sent letters fit. Risks: drafting a borrower's notice-of-error letter must stay template-based to avoid UPL, and VA disability data is sensitive. Both are handled. Redesign: Base letter templates on CFPB-published sample language. Keep VA disability status encrypted with field-level access and never exported. / keep 7/10: Past-client CRM features are drips and refi alerts. I know of no originator tool that tracks refunds owed (payoff escrow, tolerance cures, PMI premiums) against statutory clocks. It needs Verified Loan Facts uploads to work. Redesign: Make it the home for Reg X letter drafting, absorbing Hold-for-You, and share the servicer table with Coverage Guard. / keep 6/10: The rules are few and stable (training knowledge, verify: escrow balance refund within 20 days of payoff under 1024.34(b); unearned PMI premium refund within 45 days under HPA; tolerance cure within 60 days of consummation under 1026.19(f)(2)(v); force-placed refund within 15 days under 1024.37(g)), so it is a cheap code-only clock build. Data: CD and LE come from the POS, but the old servicer's escrow balance needs a payoff statement or borrower upload that MLOs often don't have. Most refunds arrive on time, so the value is mainly a legitimate service touch and goodwill, not money recovered. The tolerance-cure card before funding is the piece with real MLO time and cost value. Redesign: v1: the pre-funding LE-to-CD tolerance diff card plus the payoff escrow and PMI refund clocks, with one service reminder each. Add the drafted Reg X letter only when a clock passes. Defer the VA and FHA credit explainers.
- **Sources:** https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-C/section-1024.34 (Reg X payoff escrow refund; not opened: host blocked this session), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-B/section-1024.17 (escrow surplus refund; repo-cited in market survey), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/19/ (TRID tolerance cure 1026.19(f)(2)(v); not opened), https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/12/4902 (Homeowners Protection Act unearned premium refund; not opened), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-C/section-1024.37 (force-placed overlap refund; not opened), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1024/35/ (notice of error; repo-cited), https://www.hud.gov/program_offices/housing/sfh/handbook_4000-1 (FHA UFMIP refund credit; not opened, MED), https://www.benefits.va.gov/homeloans/ (VA funding fee refund on retroactive rating; not opened, MED)


## Reset Radar: ARM first resets and buydown step-ups, computed from the borrower's own note (round 1, high impact, ~3 weeks)

**From the CD's Adjustable Interest Rate and Projected Payments tables, plus the published index, Command Center works out each past client's contractual rate reset or buydown step-up months ahead and explains it before the servicer's notice arrives.**

- **Problem:** Five-year ARMs (5/1 and 5/6 SOFR) originated in 2021–2022 reach their first adjustment in 2026–2027. Temporary 2-1 and 1-0 buydowns, widely used in 2023–2025, step up at months 13 and 25. [MED; ARM share and buydown volumes to verify against MBA data.] [HIGH] Reg Z 1026.20(d) requires the servicer to send an initial ARM adjustment notice 210–240 days before the first payment at the new rate, and [HIGH] 1026.20(c) requires 60–120 days' notice of later payment changes. Those notices are compliance mail written for the servicer, and the originating MLO is not in the loop. A borrower who is surprised by a reset blames the lender who sold the loan, then refinances with whoever calls first. That caller may be pushing a loan that doesn't pay back, inside VA or FHA seasoning or the wholesaler's EPO window.
- **How it works:** Data: the D6 POS fields or the uploaded CD. The Second Look parser gets new fields for the CD page-4 Adjustable Interest Rate table (index, margin, first change date, initial, periodic and lifetime caps, floor, rounding) and the Projected Payments table, plus the buydown schedule. A nightly job pulls the published index (30-day average SOFR from the New York Fed, or 1-year CMT from the Federal Reserve H.15 release for older ARMs). Code, never the AI, computes the contract result: index plus margin, rounded per the note and bounded by the caps, and the resulting payment on the amortized balance (lib/pricing amortization). Triggers: 270 days before an ARM's first change date; month 10 of a buydown (the existing temporary-buydown calculator content explains each year's payment). What the MLO sees: a Today card ('Luis's 5/6 ARM resets in May 2027. Under his note at today's index: about 7.1%, about +$410/month. The servicer notice arrives about Sept 2026.') with a service-only script. What the borrower gets: a private page titled 'How your loan's own formula works', showing the index source and date, margin, caps and the estimated range at the caps, with 'your servicer's notice is the official figure'. Options are listed neutrally (keep, prepay, recast, refinance). Refinance appears only as a dated priced snapshot with APR, break-even and the not-a-commitment line, only with written marketing consent, and only after the survey's Churn Guard seasoning and EPO check passes. M8 gets a read-only tool, get_reset_projection. The guard lets M8 speak a contract-formula figure only when it matches that tool's output and carries 'estimate under your existing note, not an offer of credit'.
- **Technologies:** lib/second-look parser with new CD AIR and Projected Payments table fields (the current schema has no ARM fields), New York Fed SOFR averages (public reference-rate data), Federal Reserve H.15 or Treasury CMT series, lib/pricing amortization and the existing temporary-buydown calculator (lib/content/calculators.ts), lib/m8/guard.ts extended with a 'contract result' number class tied to tool output, D3 send gate (service and marketing purposes separated), QStash nightly index pull and dated triggers, Daily priced snapshots for the optional refinance column
- **Why it's new:** Servicers mail the Reg Z adjustment notices. Homebot reports equity and home value, not note mechanics. Per the market survey, mortgage CRMs run date-based drips and generic refi alerts, Bonzo and TrustEngine alerts lean on credit data, and the build plan's refi ripeness (5.4) and the D5 'refinance look' compare a fixed note rate against today's snapshot without modelling a contractual reset. I found no originator tool that parses the borrower's own AIR table and pre-explains the reset. This is from the survey and training knowledge; fresh search was unavailable this session, so treat it as unverified.
- **Compliance design:** The projected figure is the borrower's existing contract term computed by code, not a new rate offer. Even so, it goes through the guard as a distinct 'contract result' class, with its source, date and 'not an offer' label [COUNSEL: confirm it is not a Reg Z 1026.24 advertisement and fits the 'no rate outside a priced result' rule as written]. Any new-loan rate comes only from a priced result. Reset explanations are a service purpose. Any refinance mention switches the touch to marketing, which needs written consent for M8 calls and texts, passes DNC, quiet hours and caps, and is a separate play version. Anti-churn: Churn Guard's VA IRRRL 210-day and 36-month recoupment rules, FHA net tangible benefit and EPO windows suppress refinance content when it doesn't pay back. Fair lending: every ARM or buydown in the book gets the same treatment, with no demographic or tract input. HPPA: no credit data. LO comp: refinance pay is set by the sponsor's flat plan, never by product. GLBA: as Refund Radar.
- **Moat:** Mostly a timing and data-depth moat. Once the CD AIR parser and the index-and-rounding table exist, every new ARM closing on the platform is covered automatically from day one. The table of index sources, rounding and lookback conventions grows with members' uploads, and each new note format seen is reviewed once and then reused network-wide. Value peaks in the 2026–2028 reset wave, which is now.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: A contract-formula projection for the borrower's existing loan is not an offer of credit or an advertisement (individual communication), but it must clear the house 'no rate outside a priced result' rule, which needs a counsel-approved exception class. Separately, wholesale broker agreements often bar soliciting refinance of loans the broker placed within an EPO or non-solicit window. Churn Guard partly covers this. A projection that conflicts with the servicer's 1026.20(c)/(d) notice could mislead, which is handled with a label. Redesign: Have counsel codify the 'contract result' guard class. Suppress any refinance mention while the wholesale non-solicit or EPO window for that lender is open, read from the lender table. / drop 4/10: ARM-adjustment triggers already ship in mortgage CRMs that use LOS or servicing data (Surefire, Total Expert). The only new part is parsing the CD's Adjustable Interest Rate table, which is a parser feature. Redesign: Make it a parser plus trigger inside Verified Loan Facts, with the contract-formula guard class. / keep 5/10: The data is free and good: 30-day average SOFR from the New York Fed and H.15 CMT, plus the CD AIR table for the MLO's own closings. But the eligible book is small: ARMs were a single-digit-to-low-teens share of recent originations (recalled), so a typical MLO has few. Servicers must send the initial adjustment notice 210 to 240 days before the first new payment (1026.20(d)), so a 270-day trigger beats it by only about a month. Buydown step-ups are more numerous and simpler to compute. The CD AIR parser is an extension of Second Look. Redesign: Start with 2-1 and 1-0 buydown step-up reminders from the CD and temporary-buydown data (more volume, trivial math). Add ARM reset projections with the get_reset_projection tool. Move the ARM trigger to about 12 months out.
- **Sources:** https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/20/ (Reg Z 1026.20(c) and (d) ARM notices; not opened this session), https://www.newyorkfed.org/markets/reference-rates/sofr-averages-and-index (SOFR averages; host blocked this session), https://www.federalreserve.gov/releases/h15/ (CMT series; not opened), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/38/ (CD AIR and Projected Payments tables; not opened), https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/38/3709 (VA anti-churn; repo market survey), /home/user/RATEM8/lib/content/calculators.ts (existing temporary-buydown calculator), /home/user/RATEM8/lib/second-look/schema.ts (needs AIR fields)


## Coverage Guard: beat the force-placed insurance clock after a non-renewal (round 1, medium impact, ~4 weeks)

**When a past client's homeowners policy is non-renewed or lapses, M8 explains the Reg X 1024.37 timeline, builds the evidence-of-coverage packet for the servicer, points to the state's FAIR Plan and insurance department, and tracks the refund. No carrier is recommended and nobody is paid.**

- **Problem:** In California, major carriers paused or limited new policies from 2023, and FAIR Plan reliance grew [MED, market survey]. Non-renewals elsewhere follow wildfire and storm risk. When coverage lapses, the servicer may buy force-placed (lender-placed) insurance, which typically costs far more and protects the lender's interest, not the borrower's contents or liability [MED]. Reg X gives the borrower a clock [HIGH]: (1) an initial notice at least 45 days before any charge; (2) a reminder at least 30 days after that and at least 15 days before the charge; (3) cancellation and an overlap refund within 15 days of receiving evidence of coverage (1024.37). For escrowed borrowers, Reg X 1024.17(k)(5) generally requires the servicer to advance funds to keep the borrower's own policy rather than force-place [HIGH-MED; COUNSEL to confirm the conditions]. Borrowers don't know any of this, payments jump, and an escrow shortage can spiral into delinquency. The originating MLO hears about it last.
- **How it works:** Data: the declarations page captured at closing (policy anniversary, mortgagee clause, carrier name, used only to tell the borrower their own dates), borrower uploads (non-renewal letter, servicer's 45-day letter), and what the borrower tells M8. Triggers: a Today card and service reminder 75 days before the policy anniversary ('if you've had a non-renewal notice, tap here'); an upload or statement of a non-renewal or force-place letter. What the system does: (1) Reads the letter (Second Look parser, new document type) and lays out the borrower's own clock, with dates from the letter. (2) Explains in plain language what evidence the servicer needs: a declarations page with the mortgagee clause and loan number, sent to the servicer's insurance-processing address from the shared servicer table. (3) Shows neutral public paths from a state table in config: the state insurance department consumer line, the FAIR Plan where one exists (CA, OR, WA, and the Texas FAIR Plan Association plus TWIA for coastal wind; AZ marked VERIFY), and 'any licensed agent you choose'. (4) Generates the packet and a cover letter for the borrower to send. (5) Hands off to Refund Radar's 15-day refund clock. (6) For escrowed loans, adds a 1024.17(k)(5) note. What the MLO does: gets a private card, calls personally, and never names a carrier. What the borrower experiences: their own loan officer helping them keep their house insured at a normal price.
- **Technologies:** lib/second-look parser with non-renewal and force-place notice document types, State insurance-resources table in config (effective-dated, cited, counsel_status), on the D3 rules-table pattern, Shared servicer insurance-processing address table, PDF packet generation, QStash timers chained into Refund Radar, D3 send gate, service purpose, M8 chat and voice under the guard, with an insurance-topic refusal list (no carrier, coverage or price advice)
- **Why it's new:** Embedded agencies (Matic, Covered and others per the survey) sell policies and earn commissions, which is a RESPA 8 and producer-licensing path an MLO can't take. Servicers send the force-place notices, and Cotality sells servicers tools to cut call volume. The repo's Escrow Shock Shield (idea 10) forecasts premium increases, and the survey's Insurance Readiness covers the binder before closing. Neither handles a lapse or a force-place after closing. I found no originator-side tool that runs the borrower's 1024.37 defense; this rests on the survey and training knowledge, since fresh search was unavailable.
- **Compliance design:** Insurance is a settlement service under Reg X 1024.2, so RESPA 8 applies: no value is taken from any insurer, agency or FAIR Plan, ever. That is enforced by the co-marketing ledger's hard block, and the vendor table has no payee field. No insurance producer activity: M8 and the MLO never recommend, solicit or negotiate coverage or price, and the copy says 'we explain the servicer's rules, not insurance' [COUNSEL: producer-licensing wording per state]. Service-only touches. Triggers come only from the borrower's own documents and dates. Fair lending: wildfire, flood or climate scores are never used to select, rank or deprioritize anyone. Every past client gets the same 75-day reminder regardless of location, which avoids the redlining-proxy risk flagged in the survey. TCPA: service purpose through the gate. GLBA: uploads deleted after extraction, reads audited. No rate content.
- **Moat:** The servicer insurance-address table and the state resources table are maintained once and used by every member. Each upload adds or corrects procedural facts, with no NPI. Members in CA, TX and other stressed markets keep the FAIR Plan and department rows current through counsel-reviewed rule packs. That reference data improves with every member and every season.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: It is compliant if it stays in Reg X explanation. The risk is state producer laws that define 'solicit' or 'transact' broadly: pointing to specific carriers or agencies, or comparing price, would cross the line. The design forbids both. 1024.37 timing (45-day notice, 15-day reminder, 15-day cancel and refund) matches training knowledge. Redesign: Have the state table list only government resources and the FAIR Plan, never private carriers or agencies. Make the copy and guard block any carrier names other than the borrower's own current carrier. / keep 6/10: Embedded insurance agencies sell policies, and servicers send force-place notices. I know of no originator-side, no-commission defense against force-placed insurance. It is a narrow but timely problem in CA and TX. Redesign: Merge it with Refund Radar as one 'servicer protections' module that shares the servicer address table. / drop 4/10: Real pain in CA and other stressed markets, but rare per MLO. It touches producer-licensing wording in every state, and it needs a servicer insurance-address table no one has. Its cheap parts, the policy-anniversary reminder and the evidence-packet checklist, fit better as items in Year-One Map and Refund Radar. Redesign: Fold the 75-day policy-renewal reminder and the 'what your servicer needs' checklist into Year-One Map. Hand off the 15-day refund clock to Refund Radar. Keep state FAIR Plan and insurance-department links as static config.
- **Sources:** https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-C/section-1024.37 (force-placed insurance notices and refund; host blocked this session), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-B/section-1024.17 (1024.17(k)(5) escrow advance; not opened), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-A/section-1024.2 (insurance as a settlement service; repo market survey), https://www.insurance.ca.gov/ (California market conditions; market survey, not opened), https://www.cfpfairplan.org/ (California FAIR Plan; not opened, verify), https://www.texasfairplan.org/ and https://www.twia.org/ (Texas; not opened, verify)


## Year-One Map: the purchase-year schedule generated at closing (round 1, high impact, ~5 weeks)

**At closing, Command Center turns the CD plus a county rules table into a dated first-13-months plan for each buyer: homestead filing, the California supplemental tax bill, the seller's-exemption escrow shortage, servicing-transfer protections, the first escrow analysis, and enrolling in the county's free deed-fraud alert. Each step reaches the borrower as a service message on its date.**

- **Problem:** The first year of ownership has predictable shocks that buyers aren't warned about. California: purchases trigger supplemental property tax bills, which are mailed to the owner and often not paid from escrow, so new owners miss them [MED-HIGH]. The homeowners' exemption needs a filing [MED: $7,000 of assessed value, Feb 15 deadline for the full amount]. Texas: escrow at closing is often set from the seller's homestead-exempted taxes, so the buyer's first analysis shows a shortage once the exemption drops off. The buyer must file their own homestead exemption [MED: by April 30 of the tax year]. Washington, Oregon and Arizona have their own exemption and deferral filings [VERIFY per county]. Loans are often sold or transferred in the first months. Reg X 1024.33 requires a transfer notice at least 15 days before the transfer and within 15 days after from the new servicer, plus a 60-day window in which a payment sent to the old servicer can't be treated as late [HIGH]. Deed and seller-impersonation fraud is rising, and many county recorders run free owner notification services (for example LA County's Property Document Notification Service and Maricopa County Recorder notifications) [MED, verify per county]. Today buyers learn these things by getting hurt.
- **How it works:** Data: the closing milestone from D6, or an uploaded CD (property county, escrowed items, tax and insurance figures, servicer), plus a county and state rules table in config. Each row carries the rule, deadline formula, official link, citation, effective_from and effective_to, and counsel_status, on the D3 effective-dated pattern. Trigger: the loan reaches Closed (or the CD is uploaded). The system generates a per-loan schedule. Example for a Harris County, TX buyer: homestead application window and link; 'your escrow was set on the seller's exempt taxes, expect an analysis around month 12, here's the estimated gap' (code computed from CD figures and the county's published rates); first-payment and transfer-letter explainer with the 60-day protection; 'enrol in the county's free recording alert, takes 2 minutes' (the borrower enrols themselves on the county site); first policy renewal date, which feeds Coverage Guard; PMI dates, which feed PMI Exit Concierge. What the borrower sees: a private 'Your first year' page with checkboxes, and one service message per item on its date through the send gate (email by default, text only with permission on file). What the MLO sees: nothing until the borrower asks for help, misses a deadline they asked to be reminded of, or reports a recording alert. A recording alert gets an immediate Today card plus a script pointing to the county, the title insurer on the owner's policy and law enforcement. Reuse: the same table powers the Escrow Shock Shield (idea 10) for the whole book, but Year-One Map is the deterministic purchase-year schedule built from each CD.
- **Technologies:** County and state rules table in config (effective-dated, cited, counsel-signed), the D3 rules pattern extended to property-tax and recorder rows, D6 POS closed milestone or lib/second-look CD parsing, Escrow projection math (lib/pricing amortization plus published county tax rates), QStash dated jobs, D3 send gate, service purpose, Borrower portal page (the consent passport link), County recorder notification links (borrower self-enrolment; the platform never subscribes for them)
- **Why it's new:** Title companies and some brokerages send one 'file your homestead' postcard. Paid 'home title lock' monitoring services sell subscriptions. Servicers send transfer letters. Homebot sends equity emails. The repo ideas and survey cover reassessment warnings for the whole book (Escrow Shock Shield), jurisdiction-wide news touches (Regulatory-Moment Calendar) and servicer changes over the life of the loan (Servicer Watch). None generates a per-closing, county-specific, dated first-year schedule from the CD, and none walks the buyer through free county fraud alerts. That conclusion rests on the survey and training knowledge; fresh search was unavailable this session.
- **Compliance design:** Service messages only, with no rate and no refinance content. No tax or legal advice: every item links the assessor's or recorder's official page, with 'confirm with your county'. Exemptions for seniors, veterans or people with disabilities are listed to everyone in the county as the published list and never targeted by age, disability or veteran status (ECOA and FHA protected classes). Fair lending: an identical schedule for every closing in a county. Public records: the borrower enrols their own property in recorder alerts. The platform pulls no owner-name lists, which keeps it clear of Washington's RCW 42.56.070 commercial-use bar flagged in the survey. TCPA: email default; texts only with permission on file, through the gate. GLBA: CD data stays in-tenant, reads audited. RESPA: no title or insurance vendor pays anything; the owner's title insurer is named only because it appears on the borrower's own policy.
- **Moat:** The county rules table is the moat. Each new member in a new county triggers one counsel-reviewed county row, which then serves every future closing there. With members nationwide, the platform holds the only maintained, cited, effective-dated purchase-year table across thousands of counties. A large lender could build it, but an MLO-owned network builds it as members join, and members fund the counsel review collectively. Every closing then starts a year of useful, permitted contact.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: There is no real violation: service messages with official links, identical schedules per county, and borrower-enrolled fraud alerts. Minor risks: tax-advice framing for estimated escrow gaps, and fair-housing sensitivity if senior, disability or veteran exemptions are surfaced selectively, which the design forbids. Redesign: Label every estimate 'estimate from your CD and the county's published rate'. Show the exemptions list in full to every closing in the county. / drop 4/10: Title companies and lenders send homestead reminders, Homebot covers ongoing post-close touches, and counties offer free recording alerts. A per-closing dated schedule is a modest step, and a county rules table across thousands of counties is a big upkeep load. Redesign: Fold it into Escrow Shock Shield and Refund Radar as post-closing schedule items, covering the five launch states only. / keep 5/10: The county rules table is the cost, and in the five live states the payoff is uneven. Texas homestead and California supplemental tax bills are high-value. WA, OR and AZ have little general homestead filing (mostly senior or veteran programs; recalled, not verified). National 'thousands of counties' upkeep is unrealistic for a small team. Title companies already send homestead reminders in some markets. Redesign: v1: state-level rows (TX homestead, CA supplemental bill and Prop 13 notes, first escrow analysis estimate from CD figures, servicing-transfer 60-day protection, policy renewal from Coverage Guard). Add county rows only for the top counties by member closings. County recorder-alert links go in when a member asks.
- **Sources:** https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-C/section-1024.33 (servicing transfer notices and 60-day protection; not opened, host blocked), https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/supplemental.htm (California supplemental assessments; not opened, verify), https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/homeowners_exemption.htm (California homeowners' exemption; not opened, verify), https://comptroller.texas.gov/taxes/property-tax/exemptions/ (Texas homestead; repo market survey), https://www.lavote.gov/home/records/property-document-recording/property-document-notification-service (LA County PDNS; not opened, verify), https://recorder.maricopa.gov/ (Maricopa County Recorder notification; not opened, verify), https://app.leg.wa.gov/RCW/default.aspx?cite=42.56.070 (WA public-records commercial-use bar; market survey)


## Next Home Plan: one coordinator for a past client's two-transaction move, with the recast after the sale (round 1, high impact, ~6 weeks)

**When a past client says they want to move, Command Center lays out three honest paths on their real loan (sell first, buy first with bridge or HELOC, keep and rent), runs one timeline across both transactions, and after the old home sells, shows the recast that lowers the new payment.**

- **Problem:** A past client's next move is the most valuable lifecycle event, since it means a purchase loan, often a sale and sometimes a second lien. It is also the hardest decision while about 80% of mortgaged owners hold rates below 6% [MED, survey citing FHFA NMDB analyses]. The choices interact: whether the departing home's payment counts against the new loan, whether rental income from the old home can be used (Fannie Mae departing-residence rules, commonly a lease plus a 25% vacancy factor [MED, verify current Selling Guide B3-3.1-08 and B3-6-06]), whether a HELOC or bridge loan funds the down payment, and whether the new loan can be recast after the sale (conventional loans at servicer discretion with a minimum and a fee; FHA and VA generally not recastable [MED]). Buy-before-you-sell firms sell their own product, the agent picks the lender, and the client's own MLO, who holds the real loan facts, is not in the picture.
- **How it works:** Triggers are borrower-stated only: an M8 chat or voice request, a Time Capsule (idea 6), a Relationship Memory fact the MLO approved (idea 3), or the MLO's own note after a conversation. RETR listing data is not an automated trigger, because RETR's 2026-09-17 terms bar rules engines and automated processes; it can only be shown to the MLO for a personal call. Inputs: Verified Loan Facts (idea 4) for the current loan, a labelled equity estimate, today's priced snapshot for the target purchase scenario, and a guidelines table in config (departing-residence PITI treatment, rental-income rules, occupancy expectations) with citations. The planner (deterministic code) builds three columns. (1) Sell first: net proceeds estimate, one move, contingent-offer note. (2) Buy first: total payment carried during the overlap, financed by a HELOC on the current home or a bridge loan. The HELOC side stays a neutral explainer with no numbers until a panel program is display-reviewed (CLAUDE.md §4b). After the sale, the planner adds a recast option on the new loan: lump sum in, re-amortized payment out, with the servicer's minimum and fee from the shared recast table. (3) Keep and rent: payment against market rent (borrower-entered), landlord-insurance switch reminder, and how rental income may be counted. Assumption Desk is linked if the current loan is FHA, VA or USDA. One dual timeline covers both contracts' key dates, contingencies, closing order and the recast date. Status updates go to both agents only with the borrower's per-agent authorization. What the MLO sees: 'Priya wants to move up in spring. Plan built. Buy-first carries about $6,900/month for an estimated 60 days.' What the borrower gets: a private plan page they can share with their agent, with every rate dated, priced and paired with APR and the not-a-commitment line. If the destination is outside the MLO's licensed states, Relay (idea 11) offers the borrower-chosen handoff.
- **Technologies:** Verified Loan Facts (CD or statement parsing; Plaid later), Daily priced snapshots and lib/pricing build, apr and antiSteering for the purchase scenario, Guidelines table in config (departing residence, rental income, occupancy), cited and counsel-reviewed, Shared servicer recast-policy table (minimum, fee, eligible investors), Dual-transaction timeline on the D6 POS milestones, Borrower-authorized agent status (Agent Line pattern, idea 8), lib/routing.ts for licensing and Relay, M8 chat and voice under the guard
- **Why it's new:** Homebot shows 'buying power' from public-record value. Buy-before-sell companies (Knock, Flyhomes, Orchard) sell their own bridge or cash-offer product, so they aren't neutral. iBuyer-style tools sit with agents. The repo's Keep-Your-Low-Rate Equity Planner (idea 5) compares HELOC against cash-out for a stay-put owner, and the survey's Assumption Desk serves a seller with an assumable loan. Neither plans the move itself, the departing-residence treatment or the recast after the sale. I found no originator tool that does this on the client's real first lien. This rests on the survey and training knowledge; fresh search was unavailable this session, and the vendor names are from memory.
- **Compliance design:** Financing a new home is marketing. M8 calls and texts need written marketing consent through the D3 gate; otherwise the MLO calls by hand. No rate outside a priced result. HELOC numbers stay hidden until a program is display-cleared. Anti-steering: all three paths are always shown with neutral, published criteria. Reg Z 1026.36(d) doesn't cover HELOCs, so the sponsor must attest that the MLO's pay doesn't vary between HELOC, bridge and first-lien options [COUNSEL]. Occupancy: the planner never suggests buying 'as primary' with intent to rent, and the copy states occupancy rules plainly. Fair lending: no school, neighborhood or demographic inputs, and destination areas are never filtered. RESPA 8: agent updates go to every agent on the file equally, with nothing of value to any agent. Licensing: only licensed states; otherwise Relay. No tax advice: the Section 121 exclusion is mentioned only as 'ask your tax professional'. HPPA: no credit data. RETR data is display-only, per its terms.
- **Moat:** The recast-policy and departing-residence tables are shared reference data that members improve with each move they complete. More importantly, the plan page is the borrower's own artifact, built on loan facts only their MLO holds. An agent can't recreate it with a rate sheet, so every move on the platform makes the borrower-MLO bond harder for a competitor to break. That compounds with tenure: a client on their third move with the same MLO.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: It is compliant: borrower-stated triggers only, RETR display-only, HELOC numbers gated, and a sponsor attestation for HELOC comp (1026.36(d) doesn't reach open-end). Risks: the keep-and-rent column is near occupancy-fraud territory, though the design addresses it, and financing outreach is marketing that needs PEWC for M8 touches. It duplicates Move-Up Sequencer. Redesign: Absorb Move-Up Sequencer's month-by-month cash table and Texas homestead checks. Require a signed occupancy-intent statement before any 'keep and rent' path is priced as primary. / keep 6/10: Parts are covered by Mortgage Coach, Homebot buying power and the buy-before-you-sell firms' own calculators. Planning on the client's real first lien with a recast after the sale is a moderately new combination. It duplicates Move-Up Sequencer. Redesign: Keep this as the merged idea, absorbing Move-Up Sequencer's cash timeline and Texas homestead rules. / keep 6/10: Move-up demand from locked-in owners is real, and it produces purchase plus bridge or HELOC business. Triggers are borrower-stated, so volume depends on Relationship Memory and Time Capsule. Costs: the recast table by servicer is hard to source (needs member reporting), HELOC numbers stay hidden until review (weakening buy-first), and the dual-contract timeline duplicates Deal Clock. Redesign: Absorb Move-Up Sequencer. v1 is three columns with deterministic math and dated priced examples, plus a recast estimate using a generic 'ask your servicer' fee field. Reuse Deal Clock for the timeline. Build a shared recast table only from member-verified rows.
- **Sources:** https://selling-guide.fanniemae.com/ (departing residence and rental income, B3-3.1-08 and B3-6-06; not opened, verify), https://www.fhfa.gov/data/national-mortgage-database-aggregate-statistics (rate lock-in; repo market survey, MED), https://retr.app/terms (RETR ban on automated processes; market survey), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/36/ (LO comp scope excludes HELOCs; market survey), /home/user/RATEM8/CLAUDE.md §4b (HELOC programs not yet display-reviewed), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (ideas 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 11 it connects to)


## Hardship Lifeline: the loan officer who shows up when a client falls behind (round 1, medium impact, ~3 weeks)

**When a past client tells M8 or their MLO they are struggling, Command Center pauses every marketing play to them, explains their Reg X loss-mitigation protections in plain language, connects them to a free HUD-approved counselor, screens rescue-scam pitches, and schedules a service-only check-in for when they are back on their feet.**

- **Problem:** When a client has a job loss, illness or payment trouble, the originator usually disappears, or worse, keeps sending refinance and cash-out pitches. Borrowers don't know the protections they have [HIGH]: early-intervention live contact by day 36 and written notice by day 45 of delinquency (Reg X 1024.39); generally no first foreclosure filing until the loan is more than 120 days delinquent; and evaluation for every option when a complete loss-mitigation application arrives more than 37 days before a sale (1024.41). Foreclosure-rescue scams target exactly these homeowners. Reg O (12 CFR part 1015, the MARS rule) bars most advance fees for mortgage assistance relief [HIGH]. HUD-approved housing counselors are free. No originator tool turns this into a moment of trust.
- **How it works:** Trigger: the borrower says it only, in M8 chat or voice ('I lost my job', 'I'm behind'), to the MLO, or in a reply text. Nothing is inferred from data, and there is no delinquency feed or credit data. What the system does: (1) An immediate suppression flag pauses every marketing play and refi card for that contact for 12 months unless the borrower asks for options. (2) M8 gives a neutral explainer: call the servicer, the 36-, 45-, 120- and 37-day facts, the documents a loss-mitigation application usually needs, and that FHA, VA, USDA and GSE loans have their own option sets, with 'your servicer decides'. (3) A warm handoff to a HUD-approved counselor near them, from HUD's public counselor search, by link or a three-way call if the borrower wants. (4) Scam shield: the borrower forwards a solicitation, and M8 checks it against Reg O red flags (upfront fees, 'stop paying your lender', 'guaranteed modification', missing required disclosures) and the state foreclosure-consultant rules table, and says plainly what legitimate help looks like. (5) A state foreclosure-process row (judicial or nonjudicial and typical steps for WA, OR, AZ, CA, TX) is shown only on request, with an attorney referral link to the state bar. What the MLO sees: a private Today card ('Dana reached out about a hardship. Marketing paused. Counselor link sent. Suggested: a personal call, no product talk.') with a human script that promises nothing. Later the borrower can say 'we're back on track', which schedules one service-only check-in and a review of their options, with marketing resuming only with fresh consent. The borrower gets their own loan officer helping them, free, at the worst moment.
- **Technologies:** M8 chat and voice with a hardship intent tag and a narrow explainer tool set under the guard, Play suppression flag enforced in the D3 send gate (a new gate rule: hardship_pause), HUD-approved housing counselor public search (link-out or open data), Reg O red-flag checklist and state foreclosure-consultant rules table in config, lib/second-look parser for forwarded solicitations (data, never instructions), Twilio three-way call for an optional counselor warm handoff, Neon sensitive-fact storage excluded from every ranking model
- **Why it's new:** Servicers run loss mitigation. Nonprofit counselors and HUD's tools serve homeowners directly. Per the market survey, mortgage CRMs and AI-voice vendors are built to keep contacting the book. Nothing in the survey or the repo's 44-idea review protects a struggling past client from the originator's own automation, or connects them to free help. A send-gate rule that pauses marketing on a borrower-stated hardship is, as far as this session could tell, unoffered. This is from the survey and training knowledge; fresh search was unavailable.
- **Compliance design:** Reg O / MARS: the platform and MLO never negotiate with the servicer for the borrower, never charge anything, and never say or imply they can get a modification, forbearance or foreclosure delay. They give general information and refer to free HUD-approved counselors and the servicer [COUNSEL: Reg O 1015 coverage and exemptions; state foreclosure-consultant laws such as California Civil Code 2945]. Hardship is borrower-stated only and never inferred, with no credit or delinquency data (HPPA and FCRA clean). It is stored as a sensitive fact, never a ranking, scoring or outreach input, and only ever used to suppress marketing. That rules out targeting distressed borrowers and avoids a fair-lending proxy, since hardship correlates with protected traits. TCPA: inbound, or service-only after the borrower asks. Marketing stays paused and resumes only with fresh written consent. No rate. GLBA: audited reads; the counselor handoff shares nothing without the borrower's explicit tap. RESPA: no value to or from counselors.
- **Moat:** Trust compounds. A borrower helped through a hardship by their own loan officer is the most loyal client in the book, and only a platform whose members own the relationship (not a servicer or a lead seller) has a reason to build this. The state foreclosure-consultant and process tables are maintained in members' collectively funded counsel rule packs. The suppression rule is also a selling point to sponsors' compliance teams: provable protection against AI or automation pitching distressed homeowners, the failure pattern in the 2026 AI-voice suits the survey lists.
- **Reviews:** keep 9/10: From training knowledge, the Reg O / MARS definition (12 CFR 1015.2) is limited to services offered 'in exchange for consideration', and California's foreclosure-consultant statute (Civ. Code 2945) turns on compensation. A free, information-and-referral service stays outside both if no fee and no later solicitation ties back to it. The 12-month marketing suppression and borrower-stated-only trigger are strong. (Statute sites unreachable; verify.) Redesign: Add a hard rule that no refinance or product play may cite or be triggered by the hardship event, even after the suppression ends. Resume only on fresh written consent captured at least 30 days after the borrower says they're back on track. / keep 6/10: Servicers and HUD-approved counselors serve homeowners directly. Novel: a send-gate suppression rule that triggers when the borrower states a hardship, plus a rescue-scam checker on the originator side. It is low revenue but strongly trust-building. Redesign: Ship the suppression rule in D3 now (cheap). Add the explainer and scam checker as M8 content later. / keep 7/10: Cheap: a suppression flag in the gate, a static explainer, a HUD counselor link (HUD publishes a counselor search and dataset; recalled, not verified this session) and a scam-red-flags checklist. It's low frequency but high trust, and it is a strong compliance selling point to sponsors. Main cost is counsel review of the Reg O and state foreclosure-consultant wording. The five-state foreclosure process rows are small. Redesign: Build as specified, but make the scam shield a checklist M8 walks through rather than automatic document analysis in v1.
- **Sources:** https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1024/41/ (loss mitigation, 120-day and 37-day rules; not opened this session), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1024/39/ (early intervention; not opened), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1015/ (Reg O / MARS advance-fee ban; not opened), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/find-a-housing-counselor/ (HUD-approved counselor search; not opened, verify), https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=2945 (California foreclosure consultant law; not opened, verify), https://www.mondaq.com/unitedstates/consumer-law/1821332/ai-robocalls-tcpa-and-texas-converge-in-class-action (AI that kept pitching after 'no'; market survey)


## Licensed Hands: the SAFE Act activity boundary and live License Clock (round 1, high impact, ~5 weeks)

**Every message and action M8 or Command Center produces is sorted into a licensed-activity category. Anything that presents particular loan terms must trace to a named MLO who is licensed in that property's state at that moment and who signed off on the exact priced scenario with a passkey. A meter also stops any conversation before it collects the sixth piece of information that would make it a TRID application by accident.**

- **Problem:** Under the SAFE Act and Reg Z 1026.36(a)(1), taking an application, offering or negotiating terms, and presenting particular credit terms for a consumer to consider are all loan-originator activity, and the clerical or support exception covers none of them. No rule yet says who the originator is when an AI voice or chat agent presents terms for an MLO.
