Redesign: Gate any API or automation path per PPE behind a recorded written license approval in config. / keep 5/10: PPEs price whatever they are asked. Optimizing what to pull from unmet demand only matters while pricing is manual, and the value disappears once an API exists. Float Watch is close to existing lock-desk market alerts. Redesign: Build it as a small helper to C9 and the Pricing Rota, and drop it once API pricing arrives. / keep 5/10: Valuable only while pricing is a manual daily ARIVE pull. If a licensed pricing API (Optimal Blue, Lender Price) is adopted later, the set-cover planner becomes unnecessary. Demand data is thin at small scale. Float Watch is the durable, cheap part: comparing dated examples per bucket for floating files, MLO-only. Redesign: Build Float Watch now, plus a simple ranked 'most-requested missing scenarios' list instead of an optimizer. Revisit the planner only if manual pulls continue past a set date or member count.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md C7 (daily scenarios, match rules), C8.1 (fixed comp rule), C9 (LoanSifter import), D5 (refinance look), /home/user/RATEM8/CLAUDE.md §4b (pricing pipeline, tenants per sponsoring brokerage), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (Pricing Rota; Lender Pulse rejected), https://digitalmarketplace.optimalblue.com/product-overview (Broker Product & Pricing APIs, per survey), https://www2.optimalblue.com/developer (lock webhooks, per survey), Verification note: no live web check this run (search budget used up)


## Quote Ledger (round 1, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**Every rate, APR or price the MLO, an assistant or M8 gives a borrower by text, email or recorded call is captured, tied to the priced result it came from (or stopped if there isn't one), and later checked against the Loan Estimate and lock. 'Why did my rate change?' then has an answer in seconds, and price consistency can be proven.**

- **Problem:** The rule that 'an AI never quotes a rate outside a priced result' covers M8 only. Human MLOs quote informally by text and phone all day. When the LE comes in different, the MLO spends time reconstructing what was said and why it moved: lock date, appraisal LTV, credit band, a product change. Disputes and complaints turn on exactly that reconstruction. Examiners and fair-lending reviews look at whether similar scenarios got similar pricing and whether exceptions are documented. Today none of this is captured in one place.
- **How it works:** TRIGGER: any outbound text or email through D4's sendText, or any recorded and transcribed call where state recording notice was given. SYSTEM: (1) The guard's existing rate detector extracts the quoted terms: rate, APR, points or credit, product, lock period and payment. (2) Each quote must link to a priced result: a daily snapshot, or a capture of that borrower's own scenario through C9 or a PPE API, by snapshot ID and canonical input hash. A text with a rate and no linked result is soft-blocked: 'attach the pricing this came from' (one tap picks from today's captures) or remove the rate. House policy also requires the APR and the not-a-commitment line on any rate. (3) The ledger entry is append-only and hash-chained: who, when, channel, terms, source, scenario hash and the disclosures shown. (4) When the LE or lock arrives (from the LOS or Lender Mail Room), it is reconciled. If the LE's rate, points or credits differ beyond a threshold from the latest quote, a card appears with the file events that most likely explain the change (lock date, LTV after appraisal, credit band, loan amount, product) and a plain-language explanation draft for the borrower, which the MLO approves. (5) Consistency report: within one sponsor tenant, quotes on the same canonical bucket on the same day should match. Any variance needs a reason code (lender price exception, tolerance cure, lock extension, a different lender option the borrower chose). (6) Export: per borrower or date range, for a complaint response or an exam. MLO: no more reconstructing, and a nudge before sending an unpriced number. BORROWER: a clear 'what changed since we last talked' when the LE differs.
- **Technologies:** lib/m8/guard.ts rate detection reused on human outbound messages and call transcripts, lib/pricing canonical input hash and snapshot store, D4 sendText hook; Twilio call recording and transcription (where noticed by state), Append-only, hash-chained Neon ledger, LOS and Lender Mail Room LE and lock events, PDF/CSV export
- **Why it's new:** PPEs keep search history (Optimal Blue, Loansifter), LOSs record the lock and the LE, and CRMs store message threads. The prior research found none that ties an informal text or phone quote to the priced result it came from, stops an unpriced number before it's sent, and reconciles quotes against the LE for a broker MLO. Training knowledge plus the repo surveys; not re-verified this session.
- **Compliance design:** Reg Z: an individual quote to one consumer is generally not an 'advertisement' (TILA commentary on 1026.2(a)(2), training knowledge). APR plus the not-a-commitment line is LoanM8 house policy, applied the same way to people as to M8. Reg Z comp: the ledger has no compensation field. Reason codes record why pricing changed, and the plan's comp firewall keeps pay out of it. The commentary to 1026.36(d)(1) limits when an LO's compensation may be cut to fund a concession (unforeseen settlement-cost increases), and that's for [COUNSEL] to map to reason codes. Fair lending: consistency testing uses pricing factors only, with no race, ethnicity or proxy fields in the CRM. Whether to run proxy-based (BISG) disparity testing is a [COUNSEL] decision, done outside the CRM if at all. TCPA and recording: transcripts come only from calls with state-appropriate notice (the D3 recording flag). GLBA: NPI, audited reads, tenant isolation. Antitrust: consistency analysis stays within one sponsor's tenant.
- **Moat:** The ledger becomes the MLO's evidence trail and travels with their seat across sponsor changes, subject to the sponsor's record copies. The more of the day's communication runs through Command Center, the more complete and defensible it is, which makes the platform hard to leave for a CRM that only logs messages.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: It is compliant. Individual quotes are not 1026.24 advertisements (the 1026.2(a)(2) commentary on personal contacts and cost estimates). Risks: capturing quotes from human LO calls requires all-party recording consent in CA and WA and others, which is handled by the D3 flag. Reason codes for pricing concessions that cut LO comp must map to the narrow unforeseen-increase exception (comment 36(d)(1)-7). Redesign: Never infer quotes from unrecorded or un-noticed calls. Have counsel map reason codes before the consistency report goes to sponsors. / keep 7/10: PPE search history, LOS lock records and CRM threads are separate today. Tying informal quotes to their priced source, soft-blocking unpriced numbers and reconciling against the LE is not shipped by any vendor I know of. It extends the existing M8 guard to humans. Redesign: Make it the one priced-result binding service, absorbing Ad Desk's rate binding, so texts, ads and sheets share it. / keep 5/10: Reconciling quotes against the LE answers 'why did my rate change' and protects in complaints, which is valuable. But soft-blocking every human text that contains a rate until it is linked to a priced snapshot adds friction to the MLO's core habit, and with manual pricing, often no matching snapshot exists. That friction would drive MLOs to text from their personal phones, defeating the ledger. Call-transcript capture depends on recording, which many MLOs won't turn on. Redesign: Hard-link and block only for M8 and drafted messages. For human-typed texts, capture automatically, suggest a snapshot match, and log 'unpriced' without blocking. Reconcile against the LE where both exist.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/CLAUDE.md §1 (output guard: rate needs APR and not-a-commitment line), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md C7, C8.1, C9 (priced snapshots, comp rule, captures), D4 (sendText, redaction), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/interp-36/ (LO comp commentary; not opened this session), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/interp-2/ (advertisement definition commentary; not opened this session), Verification note: no live web check this run (search budget used up)


## One Entry: write-once intake with consent-safe sync (round 1, medium impact, ~4 weeks)

**What a borrower tells M8 in the prep flow becomes the LOS lead or loan (by the MLO's own Zapier action, or a standard MISMO or FNM export), so nobody re-types it. Every field has one named system of record. A phone or email change coming back from the LOS or POS never inherits marketing consent.**

- **Problem:** The same borrower facts get typed three or four times: chat or call notes, the CRM, the LOS lead, the POS application. Two-way syncs fix the typing but create a compliance bug. TCPA consent belongs to a specific person, number and purpose. When the LOS overwrites a borrower's phone, adds a co-borrower's number, or the POS sends a new email, a naive sync either copies the old consent onto the new channel or silently loses an opt-out. D2 and D4 built consent per channel and a durable opt-out list, but no sync rules yet.
- **How it works:** DATA: lib/prep/machine.ts fields (no SSN, DOB, account numbers or documents, by design), D2 contacts and consents, and LOS and POS records. TRIGGER (outbound): the borrower finishes prep in /chat?prep=1, or the MLO taps 'Start file' on a contact. SYSTEM: (1) It creates the LOS lead or loan through the MLO's own ARIVE Zapier action (create or update lead or loan, convert lead; Pro seat, per the survey), or produces a MISMO 3.4 or FNM 3.2 file for any LOS that imports one (the import support is training knowledge; verify per LOS). The borrower then completes the sensitive parts only in the secure POS. (2) A field-of-record map in config: the LOS owns loan facts; Command Center owns consent, relationship and preferences; the POS owns application data. A conflict never silently overwrites. It shows as a one-line reconcile card ('LOS has a new mobile for Dana'). (3) Consent-safe sync: a phone or email arriving from the LOS or POS becomes a new channel row. Its starting status comes from its own origin, for example 'given on the application, about this loan', which counsel may treat as service-only consent for the transaction. It never inherits marketing or AI-call consent from another number. The cc_optouts list is checked on every new channel, and an opt-out on the old number stays. Co-borrowers become separate contacts with their own consents, never merged into the primary. (4) Inbound: LOS milestones and dates flow to Deal Clock and Today. Command Center writes back only notes and tasks where the LOS allows. MLO: no re-keying, one reconcile card a week instead of mystery duplicates. BORROWER: answers once in M8 chat and isn't asked the same questions again, except what must be entered securely.
- **Technologies:** lib/prep/machine.ts prep flow, ARIVE Zapier actions (create/update lead and loan) per MLO credentials, MISMO 3.4 / FNM 3.2 export, Field-of-record map in config with reconcile cards, D2 consent tables and D4 cc_optouts, D3 send gate
- **Why it's new:** POS systems (ARIVE's POS, Floify, Blend) collect applications, and CRMs push leads by Zapier. Survey facts: Bonzo charges per hosted LOS integration, and Shape users report field-mapping issues on ARIVE's Zapier. The prior research found no mortgage CRM whose sync treats TCPA consent as belonging to the channel, so that a number arriving from the LOS starts without inherited marketing consent and co-borrowers keep separate consent. Training knowledge plus the repo surveys; not re-verified this session.
- **Compliance design:** TCPA: consent is per person, number and purpose, and a synced channel never inherits consent. Whether a number given on a loan application is prior express consent for informational calls about that transaction is a [COUNSEL] point (FCC precedent supports it for related informational calls, from training knowledge). Marketing and AI calls still need written consent through the consent passport. Opt-outs survive every sync. SAFE Act: M8 collects prep information, and the licensed MLO takes the application through the POS. Whether AI intake counts as 'taking an application' is [COUNSEL]. Prep never says 'submitted'. GLBA: no sensitive identifiers flow through the CRM, Zapier credentials belong to the MLO, every read is audited, and the export file is generated on demand and not stored. ARIVE terms: use the MLO's own credentials and ARIVE's documented Zapier route (the owner already has written OK for automation, per C7). Get written partner approval before scaling (survey: reported vendor restrictions). Fair lending: intake asks every borrower the same questions, with no demographic inputs beyond what the application legally requires, and those are collected in the POS, not the CRM.
- **Moat:** Every LOS and POS member brokers use adds a field map. The consent-safe sync rules are counsel-reviewed once and reused by everyone. As M8 handles more of members' first conversations, One Entry becomes the default way borrower data enters any LOS without re-keying, independent of which LOS vendor a sponsor picks.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: Treating consent per channel is the correct strict reading. A number given on an application supports informational calls about that transaction (2008 ACA International ruling, from training knowledge), and never marketing or AI-voice marketing. The ARIVE Zapier route must stay within ARIVE's terms. FNM 3.2 import support is declining after the URLA and MISMO 3.4 transition, which is a fact risk, not a compliance one. Redesign: Default synced numbers to 'transaction-informational' only, with marketing and AI-call consent captured separately through the passport. Prefer MISMO 3.4 export and verify FNM 3.2 per LOS. / drop 4/10: Pushing leads to the LOS through Zapier or MISMO/FNM export is commodity: ARIVE's Zapier route, Bonzo's LOS integrations. Consent that belongs to the channel and never carries over on sync is a correctness rule that D2 and D6 should already enforce, not a new product. Redesign: Write the channel-consent rule into D2 and D6 sync as a requirement with a test, and treat the prep-to-LOS push as plumbing. / keep 8/10: Re-keying is a daily cost, and the per-channel consent rule (synced numbers never inherit marketing consent, co-borrowers kept separate) is a real compliance differentiator that is cheap to enforce in D2 and D3. Dependencies: ARIVE's Zapier create-lead and create-loan actions and seat requirements (from the repo survey; not re-verified this session), and MISMO 3.4 or FNM 3.2 import support per LOS (training knowledge). Field-mapping bugs on Zapier are a support cost. Redesign: Build as specified. Start with ARIVE Zapier create-lead plus an FNM 3.2 export fallback. Make the field-of-record map and the reconcile card part of v1. Get written ARIVE partner approval before scaling past the founding members.
- **Sources:** /home/user/RATEM8/CLAUDE.md §4c (Prep with M8: never asks for SSN, DOB, account numbers; never says submitted), /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D2 (consent per channel and purpose), D4 (cc_optouts), C7 (owner's written ARIVE OK for automation), https://support.arive.com/support/solutions/articles/61000312645-how-does-zapier-integration-work- (ARIVE Zapier actions, per survey), https://supergood.ai/api-report-card/arive (vendor claim about ARIVE API restrictions; unverified), https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-64/subpart-L/section-64.1200 (TCPA consent rules; not opened this session), Verification note: no live web check this run (search budget used up; www.ecfr.gov blocked)


## Reuse Ledger: what the past client already paid for that should come off the next loan (round 2, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**Every closed loan stores what can be reused or credited on the client's next loan: title refinance or reissue credit, an existing survey, the FHA upfront MIP refund, no-appraisal streamline eligibility. Refinance break-even then uses the real, lower cost, and the borrower knows what to ask the title company for.**

- **Problem:** Refi ripeness (plan 5.4) and the Today 'refinance look' (D5) treat every closing cost as new. Past clients often qualify for credits they never claim: a title refinance or reissue discount on a prior policy, a reusable survey, a refund of part of the FHA upfront mortgage insurance premium, or streamline programs with no appraisal. Nobody tracks these from the last closing, so break-even looks worse than it is, some refinances that are worth doing never happen, and borrowers overpay. Title insurers have faced class actions alleging they failed to give reissue discounts [MED, from memory: several state suits around 2008–2012; verify].
- **How it works:** DATA: When a loan funds (POS sync, D-series pipeline), or when a past client uploads a Closing Disclosure through Verified Loan Facts (taken idea 4, used here as an input), the parser stores reuse facts in a `reuse_facts` table: lender's and owner's policy amounts and dates, the title underwriter from the CD, the survey date and type, the program (FHA, VA, conventional), UFMIP paid and the endorsement month, the appraisal date, and any VA funding fee exemption. The borrower's vault keeps copies of the policy jacket and survey, borrower-controlled and deletable. RULES: a per-state, effective-dated `reuse_rules` table like D3's rules table, with citation, effective_from and counsel_status columns. Starting rows: Texas promulgated rate rule R-8 (credit on a mortgagee policy for a refinance within a stated number of years) and the T-47/T-47.1 residential real property affidavit for reusing an existing survey when nothing changed [MED: verify against TDI's Basic Manual]; underwriter-filed reissue or short-term rates in WA, OR, AZ and CA, entered per underwriter [LOW: rate manuals to collect]; FHA-to-FHA UFMIP refund credit within 36 months on HUD's declining schedule (HUD 4000.1, refund table) [MED]; FHA streamline and VA IRRRL generally with no appraisal; VA IRRRL funding fee 0.5% and exemptions [HIGH]. TRIGGER: refi ripeness, Wave Ready (below), or the borrower asking M8 'what would refinancing cost me?'. SYSTEM: the break-even engine subtracts only credits marked 'likely' (state rule plus facts present) and shows them separately as an estimate, never netted silently. When a Loan Estimate later comes in through the Second Look or C20 parser, it checks the title lender's-policy line and flags 'no refinance credit shown; the rate manual may allow one, ask your title company' to the MLO and the borrower. MLO: sees 'Likely credits $1,1xx (estimate): R-8 title credit, survey reuse' on the card, and a priced dated example only from get_pricing. BORROWER: a 'Bring this to your title company' sheet with the prior policy attached, the rule citation, and a plain statement that they may choose any title provider. AGENT: nothing.
- **Technologies:** CD and LE parser (Second Look / C20) extended with title, survey and UFMIP fields, with zod schemas, Effective-dated Postgres rules table with citations (pattern from lib/send-gate/rules.ts), lib/pricing build and APR math for net break-even, Borrower document vault with per-file delete (Neon plus encrypted object storage), POS/LOS connector milestone 'funded' event
- **Why it's new:** CRMs (Bonzo, Shape, Total Expert, Homebot) compute refinance savings from rate and payment only. Title agents apply reissue credits only if someone asks and produces the prior policy, usually after the Loan Estimate has already gone out. Plan 5.4, D5 and the taken ideas (refund radar, reset radar, quote ledger) don't model costs the borrower already paid that can be reused. I found no MLO tool that keeps the prior title policy and survey and checks a later LE for the missing credit. Verification caveat: this session's web-search budget was exhausted and tdi.texas.gov, calhfa.ca.gov and consumerfinance.gov were outside the allowed network, so R-8, T-47 and the UFMIP refund schedule are from model knowledge (MED) and must be checked against primary text before the rules rows ship.
- **Compliance design:** RESPA Section 8: no value flows from any title company; title stays a borrower-shoppable service and the sheet says the borrower may choose any provider. No preferred title list. Reg Z/TRID: credits are labelled estimates for the MLO; the LE prepared by the lender of record controls; no tolerance promises. M8 never quotes a rate outside a priced result, and any rate shown carries APR and the not-a-commitment line. TCPA: the borrower-facing sheet goes out only through the D3 gate. If it's attached to a refinance suggestion it's marketing and needs prior express written consent; the MLO can hand-dial under D3's rules. Fair lending: it applies to every past client with the facts present, and isn't a ranking input beyond break-even. GLBA: vault documents are NPI, encrypted, audited and borrower-deletable; old-sponsor files only arrive by borrower upload. AI disclosure precedes any M8 conversation about it. No lead sold.
- **Moat:** The reuse_rules table is costly to build state by state and underwriter by underwriter, and member-funded counsel review keeps it current. Each LE uploaded across the network shows whether credits actually appeared (de-identified, counsel-cleared), which improves the 'likely' threshold. Every loan closed on the platform adds reusable facts that a later lender or CRM won't have unless the borrower brings them.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: No real violation found. The cited mechanisms exist: Texas TDI Basic Manual rate rules for mortgagee-policy refinance credit and the T-47/T-47.1 survey affidavit, the FHA UFMIP refund credit on FHA-to-FHA refinances within 3 years (HUD 4000.1), and the VA IRRRL 0.5% funding fee with exemptions. All were checked from memory only; this run's web search budget was used up. The risks are UDAAP and TRID. (1) A break-even that shows an estimated credit the LE later doesn't show can mislead the borrower about cost. (2) The 'ask your title company' prompt must not turn into a suggested title provider, which would raise a RESPA 8 issue if any title agent refers business to the MLO. (3) The vault holds prior title policies and surveys, which are GLBA NPI when tied to the loan. (4) A borrower-facing sheet attached to a refinance suggestion is telemarketing and needs PEWC. Redesign: Before production, every reuse_rules row needs a primary-source citation (the TDI Basic Manual R-rule text, the HUD 4000.1 UFMIP refund table, the underwriter rate manuals) and counsel_status=approved. Show credits only as a separate 'possible, not guaranteed' line, and never inside the APR or payment math. Keep the sheet free of any provider names except the borrower's own prior title company, which is printed on their policy. / keep 5/10: Partly refuted (from training knowledge; this run's web search budget was used up and vendor hosts such as lodestarss.com were blocked). Title and closing-cost fee engines (LodeStar, ClosingCorp SmartFees, title agents' own rate calculators) already work out reissue and refinance credits once prior-policy facts are entered. FHA streamline UFMIP refund credits are already computed in FHA Connection and the LOS. A hand-built per-state title rate table would duplicate those engines and go stale. What is new: keeping the past client's prior policy and survey in their vault, and checking a later LE for a missing credit. Redesign: Drop the home-built title rate rows and call a licensed fee engine (LodeStar or ClosingCorp) with the stored prior-policy facts. Keep only the vault fields and the 'no refinance credit shown on this LE' check, as an add-on to Verified Loan Facts and the Second Look / C20 LE parser rather than a separate desk. / drop 4/10: Economics: the dollars are real but small and mostly captured elsewhere already. FHA-to-FHA UFMIP refund credits are computed by FHA Connection when the new case number is assigned, so the LOS already applies them. Title reissue or refinance credits are applied by the title agent from its own title plant, often before the policy is issued; the MLO does not control the LE title line and cannot change it. Data needs prior CDs and policy jackets for past clients, which the book mostly lacks (only own closings via POS). Weekly use is near zero for a typical MLO. The 'likely credit' threshold needs per-underwriter rate manuals in 4 states, and nobody on the team can keep those current cheaply. Unverified this run: the Texas R-8 and T-47 details (search budget exhausted, no primary source opened). Redesign: Don't build a desk. Add 4 fields to Verified Loan Facts (prior policy date and amount, survey date, FHA endorsement month, VA funding fee exemption). Let the refi break-even card show 'ask title about a refinance or reissue credit; prior policy dated X' as a checklist line, with no dollar estimate until a TX rule row is confirmed against TDI's Basic Manual.
- **Sources:** Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md §5.4 (refi ripeness), §2, Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D5 (Today 'refinance look' uses rate gap only), Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md idea 4 (Verified Loan Facts upload path), Texas Department of Insurance title Basic Manual, rate rules incl. R-8 and survey affidavit forms T-47/T-47.1: https://www.tdi.texas.gov/title/titlem3b.html (not reachable this session; verify), HUD Single Family Housing Policy Handbook 4000.1 (UFMIP refunds, streamline refinance): https://www.hud.gov/program_offices/housing/sfh/handbook_4000-1 (not opened; MED), VA IRRRL funding fee and appraisal: https://www.benefits.va.gov/homeloans/ (not opened; HIGH on 0.5% fee), 12 CFR 1024.14 (RESPA Section 8): https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1024/subpart-B/section-1024.14


## Wave Ready: a rate ladder over the book and borrower-staged refinance files (round 2, high impact, ~5 weeks)

**Before rates dip, the MLO sees how many consented past clients become worth refinancing at each rate level. Clients who opt in keep a light 'ready file' current, with no credit pull and no TRID application. When a dated priced example crosses a client's own threshold, a queue ordered by borrower benefit sends each one a guarded, priced message the MLO approves in one tap.**

- **Problem:** Refinance demand comes in short spikes (the market survey cites refi application spikes on rate dips in September 2025 and January 2026; MED, not re-verified). A solo MLO with 300 ripe past clients can serve maybe 15–25 new files a week. Servicer recapture desks (Rocket plus Mr. Cooper) and wholesaler AI (UWM Mia) reach the borrower first, because they hold the monthly relationship and have the capacity. Rate watch (5.6) notifies one borrower; nothing helps an independent MLO prepare a whole book for a dip, decide who to serve first fairly, and keep every touch consented before the dip arrives.
- **How it works:** DATA: verified loan facts (POS for own closings, borrower uploads otherwise), Reuse Ledger credits, the churn and seasoning gate (survey opening 'Churn Guard', used here as a gate input, not re-proposed), consent records from D2 and the consent passport, and daily priced snapshots. RATE LADDER (MLO only): a nightly job recomputes, for each consented client, months-to-break-even at hypothetical rate steps (for example every 0.125 below today's dated example). It draws a histogram: 'at an example 0.5 lower, 41 clients cross a 24-month break-even; 9 are still inside VA/FHA seasoning; 6 inside an EPO window'. That lets the MLO book processing help (the licensed exception desk if on the platform) and lender capacity before the dip. READY FILE (borrower, opt-in): through M8 chat or the checkup line, after the AI disclosure, the client sets their own threshold ('tell me when payback is under 30 months'). They confirm occupancy and goals, and sign prior express written consent for this purpose through the consent passport e-signature. They may keep a few documents current in their vault (latest paystub, statement); M8 says when one ages past 60 days. It never collects SSN, and the ready file is capped so it never holds the six TRID 'application' items together (application clock idea is the sentinel, referenced not repeated). TRIGGER: get_pricing returns a dated example that crosses a client's threshold, after Reuse Ledger credits. QUEUE: ordered by borrower benefit normalized to the loan: months to break-even, then percentage payment reduction. Never by loan amount, dollar savings or anything tied to MLO pay, and the order is shown with its reason. Capacity: the MLO sets a weekly number. Clients beyond it get an honest text: 'your saved scenario crossed; [MLO] will call by Thursday; nothing is locked'. SEND: each draft goes to one-tap approval through the D3 gate, with the dated example, APR and not-a-commitment line. M8 voice calls only with AI-call PEWC, with AI and recording disclosures first. A parity report shows outreach and response by loan-amount band, so the queue can't quietly favour big loans.
- **Technologies:** Nightly precompute job (QStash) over lib/pricing build, APR and break-even, Daily priced snapshots and get_pricing (dated examples only), Consent passport e-signature for purpose-specific PEWC, Borrower vault with document freshness timers, D3 send gate and D4 Twilio texting; M8 voice runtime for consented calls, Charting in Today (rate-ladder histogram), Parity report by loan-amount band in the Compliance Center
- **Why it's new:** Rate-alert products (Mortgage Coach, Bonzo or MMI alerts, Homebot, UWM Mia's rate-watch calls) notify per borrower, using signals the lender chooses. None forecasts a whole book at hypothetical rates, collects consent and fresh documents before the dip, or orders the queue by normalized borrower benefit with a fairness report. Servicer recapture is fast but serves the servicer, not the client's own MLO. Plan 5.4 and 5.6 compute and alert for one person; this is the book-level capacity and consent layer that turns them into funded loans in a short window. Vendor descriptions here are from the repo's prior survey; nothing was re-verified this session because the search budget was exhausted.
- **Compliance design:** TCPA: the borrower opts in to the dip notification with prior express written consent ahead of time, so the touch is consented when it fires. AI calls need separate AI-call consent; any reasonable revocation is honored immediately everywhere (One Stop). Quiet hours, caps and DNC go through D3 unchanged. TRID and Reg B: the ready file never collects SSN and never holds all six TRID application items, so no Loan Estimate clock starts until the borrower chooses to apply. Reg Z 1026.36: the queue never uses loan amount, rate spread or anything tied to compensation; anti-steering options come from lib/pricing/antiSteering.ts. Fair lending and ECOA: ordering by normalized benefit avoids systematically favouring larger loans; there are no demographic or tract inputs, and the band parity report runs per model version. Anti-churn: the seasoning, VA recoupment and EPO checks block cards. HPPA: no credit data; thresholds are the borrower's own. AI: M8 never states a rate outside a priced result, with APR and the not-a-commitment line every time, and never promises a lock or closing date. GLBA: vault encrypted, audited and deletable.
- **Moat:** Each dip leaves the platform with calibrated data, per member and de-identified network-wide: response by break-even band, document freshness, capacity overflows. That improves ladder forecasts and Play Exchange. Ready files and consents belong to the borrower relationship and move with the MLO under the portable-seat terms. An enterprise CRM can't offer that to an independent MLO, and a servicer won't.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: The design holds up, with three risks. (1) TRID 1026.19(e)(2)(iii) bars a creditor from requiring verification documents before it gives an LE. If the 'ready file' is framed as needed to get a quote or a place in the queue, it breaks that rule. (2) The capacity-overflow text ('[MLO] will call by Thursday') is a timing promise, and the output guard bans promises. (3) The PEWC for the dip notification must specifically disclose artificial-voice/AI calls if M8 voice is used (FCC 2024 AI ruling; the FCC 24-84 proposal would require AI-specific consent disclosure). The parity report by loan-amount band is good, but loan amount correlates with race and income. Ordering by percentage payment reduction can still produce disparate impact and needs the fair-lending test, not only the band report. Anti-churn depends on Churn Guard being a hard gate. Redesign: State in the consent and the UI that documents are optional, and that a quote or LE is available without them at any time. Replace 'will call by Thursday' with 'your loan officer will reach out; nothing is locked'. Use a separate PEWC checkbox for AI voice calls. Run the scoring fair-lending check on queue order for each model version, and make Churn Guard and the seasoning blocks hard preconditions. / drop 4/10: Mostly refuted (training knowledge, not re-verified live). Lender and CRM analytics already score a whole database for refinance opportunity at rate triggers: ICE Surefire and MSP recapture analytics, Total Expert Insights, Sales Boomerang, Optimal Blue opportunity alerts. ICE's Mortgage Monitor regularly publishes the 'refinanceable population' at each rate level, which is the same idea as the ladder. The borrower-set threshold is already plan 5.6, and the seasoning gate is Churn Guard. The only new parts are the pre-dip ready file and the parity report, and the ready file adds TRID application-clock risk. Redesign: Fold the ladder histogram into plan 5.4 and 5.6 as an MLO view. Put the borrower threshold and consent capture in Time Capsule or the consent passport. Keep the loan-amount-band parity report as a Play Exchange fairness check. No separate product. / keep 7/10: This has the strongest economics in the batch. Refi volume comes in short windows, and an MLO who knows ahead of time how many clients cross break-even at each rate step can staff for it and convert. The ladder is cheap nightly math over facts the platform already plans to hold. Weak points: (1) the ladder is only as good as verified loan facts, which most imported books lack (rate and balance would be estimates); (2) the document-freshness 'ready file' vault adds heavy GLBA scope and borrowers rarely keep paystubs current unprompted; (3) the parity report and capacity overflow texts are extra build that a small team should defer. Competitor claims (Homebot, Bonzo, Mia rate watch) were not re-verified this run. Redesign: MVP in two parts. (a) Ladder histogram for the MLO, using only loans with verified note rate and balance, marked 'estimated' for others. (b) Borrower-set threshold alert with PEWC captured at opt-in, firing on a priced dated example after the Churn Guard gate. Defer the document vault, the capacity messaging and the parity report until a dip actually produces volume.
- **Sources:** Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md §5.4, §5.6, §2.1, §2.5, Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D2 (consent log), D3 (gate), D4 (texting), D5 (Today), Market survey in this task (homeowner lifecycle segment): refi spikes Sept 2025 and Jan 2026, lock-in share, Rocket plus Mr. Cooper recapture, UWM Mia (MED, not re-verified), 12 CFR 1026.2(a)(3)(ii) TRID application definition: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1026/subpart-A/section-1026.2, 12 CFR 1026.36(d)-(e) loan originator compensation and anti-steering: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1026/subpart-E/section-1026.36, 38 U.S.C. 3709 (VA refinance seasoning and recoupment): https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/38/3709, UWM Mia: https://www.housingwire.com/articles/uwm-mia-borrower-engagement/


## Junior Lien Keeper: down-payment-assistance seconds and PACE assessments tracked from the client's own file (round 2, medium impact, ~4 weeks)

**For past clients who bought with a housing finance agency down-payment second (forgivable, deferred or shared-appreciation), or who later took a PACE assessment, the system keeps the second lien's rules: forgiveness date, repayment triggers, subordination policy, appreciation share. Every refinance or sale conversation then starts honest, and forgiveness dates become service touches.**

- **Problem:** First-time buyers often close with HFA assistance (WSHFC, Oregon, Arizona, CalHFA MyHome and Dream For All, TSAHC/TDHCA in Texas). Those seconds can come due on sale or refinance, can need the agency's subordination (sometimes only for rate-and-term, not cash-out), and in the case of shared-appreciation programs like CalHFA Dream For All, require repaying principal plus a share of appreciation [MED from memory: about 20%, less for lower-income borrowers; verify]. MLO refinance math (plan 5.4, D5) ignores them, so an MLO can pitch a refinance that triggers repayment or loses forgiveness that's months away. That's a harmful churn risk that concentrates on lower-income borrowers. PACE assessments sit on the tax roll with lien priority, are now subject to Reg Z ability-to-repay under the CFPB's PACE rule (effective about 2026-03-01; MED, verify), and complicate GSE and FHA refinancing [MED]. Past clients rarely understand either.
- **How it works:** DATA: at funding, the POS record or the Closing Disclosure carries the second's program, amount, note date and recorded lien; a past client can also upload the DPA note or deed of trust through the vault. A `junior_liens` table holds the program ID, terms, recording reference and source. RULES: an effective-dated `dpa_programs` table per agency, with forgiveness schedule, repayment triggers (sale, refinance, cash-out, non-occupancy), subordination policy and fee, shared-appreciation formula, and citation and counsel_status. Rows are added only for programs the network actually closes. PACE rows record lien priority and GSE and FHA treatment. TRIGGERS: (1) a forgiveness milestone 60 days out creates a service card: 'Your assistance second is scheduled to be forgiven on [date]; here is how release is recorded.' (2) Any refinance card, Wave Ready crossing or move-up or sale plan for that contact is recomputed with the second: repayment or subordination outcome, appreciation share estimated from a labelled value estimate, and net benefit after it. If refinancing would forfeit forgiveness that's close, the card is suppressed with the reason, or shown with the 'wait until [date]' math. (3) For PACE, only the client's own words ('a solar contractor offered PACE') or a tax-roll assessment line on a past client's own parcel creates a card. Address-only matching, under the per-county public-records use table, never owner-name lists. SYSTEM and M8: M8 explains the program's own published terms neutrally after the AI disclosure, never recommends against the agency or a contractor, and drafts the borrower's own subordination request when a refinance goes ahead. MLO: sees the lien on the contact timeline and in every calculation. BORROWER: a private 'your assistance loan' page with dates and rules, linked to the agency's page.
- **Technologies:** CD and DPA note parser (zod) in the existing upload pipeline, Effective-dated program rules table with citations and counsel status, lib/pricing amortization plus a shared-appreciation calculator, Property value estimate vendor (labelled estimate), County tax-roll special-assessment data where licensed and allowed, address-only, D3 send gate (service purpose), QStash milestone jobs, Draft letter generation for subordination requests
- **Why it's new:** HFA servicers and the agencies track their own seconds. MLO CRMs track the first lien at best (Homebot uses public-record estimates, and Bonzo, Shape and BNTouch use date drips). The survey's 'Second-Lien Lifecycle' opening covers HELOCs, and the taken ideas cover ARM and buydown resets (reset radar), refunds and force-placed insurance. None models HFA assistance seconds or PACE inside refinance and sale math, or uses forgiveness dates as service touches. Program terms here are from model knowledge; calhfa.ca.gov and consumerfinance.gov were unreachable from this session, so every program row must be built from the agency's current guide.
- **Compliance design:** Fair lending: DPA borrowers skew lower-income, so the tool may only add service and protection. It never lowers anyone's priority, and suppression applies only where the borrower would lose value. LMI and MMCT data aren't inputs; the program comes from the borrower's own file. Anti-churn: it blocks refinance cards that forfeit near-term forgiveness unless the net math after repayment favours the borrower. TCPA: forgiveness and lien-release notices are service purpose (counsel to confirm); any refinance mention is marketing and needs prior express written consent. Reg Z 1026.36: the MLO's pay never varies by whether a second is kept or paid. PACE: neutral explanation only, no disparagement of contractors and no product recommendation; any alternative (HELOC, cash-out, FHA or Fannie energy options) appears only as priced, dated examples with APR and the not-a-commitment line. Public records: address-only matching for own past clients, under the per-county use rules (WA RCW 42.56.070). GLBA: lien documents encrypted, audited and deletable. AI disclosure first; M8 never quotes a rate outside a priced result. No lead sold.
- **Moat:** The program table covers dozens of agencies, each with yearly changes, and becomes a shared asset maintained by member-funded counsel. Every closing on the platform adds lien facts no outside CRM has, and network volume decides which agencies' rules get added first.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: The design is protective, and I found no likely violation. Four points of caution. (1) Suppressing refinance cards for DPA borrowers, who skew LMI and minority, must only ever protect value and never reduce service. A borrower who asks must always get a full answer. (2) Tax-roll PACE detection on a past client's parcel is a public-record use and must follow the county's commercial-use limits (WA RCW 42.56.070(8) bars commercial use of lists of individuals; single-address lookup for an existing client is lower risk). (3) PACE has been Reg Z credit under the CFPB's EGRRCPA 307 PACE rule, effective 2026-03-01 per my training. Its current status is not re-verified because search was exhausted. Any PACE explanation must stay neutral and must not disparage providers (UDAAP). (4) Forgiveness notices are service messages, but under the FCC's dual-purpose doctrine any refinance mention makes them telemarketing. Redesign: Write as a rule that suppression only removes MLO-initiated refinance marketing, never borrower-initiated answers. Log every suppression for the fair-lending test. Build each dpa_programs row from the agency's current guide with counsel_status before display, and keep PACE wording limited to the program's published terms plus the borrower's own document. / keep 6/10: I know of no MLO CRM or consumer tool that tracks a past client's HFA second-lien rules (forgiveness date, repayment triggers, subordination policy) after closing. Agencies and their servicers track their own seconds only, and Down Payment Resource catalogues programs, not individual borrowers' liens (training knowledge; downpaymentresource.com was unreachable). Overlaps in this batch: the PACE path overlaps Before You Sign, the subordination logic overlaps Second-Lien Desk, and the dpa_programs table overlaps Counselor and DPA Bridge. Redesign: Merge with Second-Lien Desk into one Junior Lien desk (HELOC, DPA second, PACE) with one subordination playbook and one dpa_programs table shared with the DPA Bridge. Send PACE intake through Before You Sign. Launch with the agency programs in the five licensed states only. / drop 3/10: The dpa_programs table is the whole cost. Dozens of HFA and local programs, each with yearly changes, and rows only pay off for MLOs who do heavy DPA volume. Forgiveness-date touches create goodwill but no revenue, and borrowers on forgivable seconds are the ones least likely to refinance soon. The PACE half overlaps Before You Sign and depends on tax-roll parsing per county, which is expensive and rarely triggers. Program terms are from model knowledge only. Redesign: Fold into Verified Loan Facts: store the second's program, note date, forgiveness date and subordination contact as fields. Make Churn Guard and Wave Ready subtract the repayment or block with 'wait until [date]'. Drop PACE here and leave it to Before You Sign. Add program rows only when a member's volume in that program justifies it.
- **Sources:** Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/LoanM8_MLO_Command_Center_Build_Prompt.md §2.2 (fair lending: LMI data only to add outreach), §5.4, Market survey in this task: Washington RCW 42.56.070 and King County restrictions on commercial use of public-record lists; second-lien lifecycle opening (HELOCs), CalHFA Dream For All shared appreciation loan: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/dream/index.htm (host blocked this session; terms MED from memory), Washington State Housing Finance Commission DPA programs: https://www.wshfc.org/buyers/ (not opened), CFPB Residential PACE final rule (Reg Z): https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/final-rules/residential-property-assessed-clean-energy-financing-regulation-z/ (host blocked; effective date MED), FHFA statements on PACE and GSE loans: https://www.fhfa.gov/ (not opened; MED)


## Co-Borrower Release Planner: getting the parent or co-signer off the loan when the borrower is ready (round 2, medium impact, ~3 weeks)

**For past clients whose loan has a non-occupant co-borrower (often a parent), the system tracks when the occupant borrower could likely refinance alone or get a release of liability. It plans that loan with them, freeing the co-borrower's own credit for their next mortgage, which is often a second funded loan.**

- **Problem:** First-time buyers often qualify only with a non-occupant co-borrower. Years later the occupant earns more, and the co-borrower wants off: the debt counts against their own DTI for their retirement purchase, refinance or downsizing. Options differ by program. A conventional loan generally needs a refinance; FHA and VA loans can allow a release of liability through the servicer in some cases [MED]. The decision depends on equity, the borrower's income now and today's priced options. No one tracks this from the original file, so the moment passes or the family goes to whichever lender calls first.
- **How it works:** DATA: the original file's co-borrower flags from the POS record (non-occupant co-borrower, co-signer) for own closings, or a borrower upload; no relationship is inferred or stored ('co-borrower', never 'parent'). The occupant borrower's own updated income and assets come from the borrower, through a consented ready-file-style update, never a credit pull. TRIGGER: borrower-initiated (either party asks M8), or a service check-in at a time the borrower chose (Time Capsule, taken, used as the channel). SYSTEM: (1) a program rules table: whether release of liability is possible (FHA or VA through the servicer, with steps) or a refinance is needed (conventional); (2) a solo-qualification estimate from the borrower's stated income against today's priced, dated example, labelled 'not an approval' and never presented as one; (3) the Reuse Ledger credits that lower the refinance's cost. MLO: a card 'occupant borrower may be able to carry the loan alone; co-borrower may want their own financing'. With each person's separate consent (they are separate consumers, separate consent records and separate GLBA rights), a second card for the co-borrower's own next loan. BORROWERS: each sees only their own information. Nothing about one is disclosed to the other without that person's authorization, logged per field.
- **Technologies:** POS co-borrower flags; borrower upload parser, Program rules table (release-of-liability paths), effective-dated and cited, lib/pricing dated examples; Reuse Ledger, Per-person consent and authorization records (D2 consent log extended to multi-party loans), M8 chat or checkup line with the AI disclosure
- **Why it's new:** CRMs keep one contact per loan or merge co-borrowers into a household, and the taken family transition desk covers divorce, death and estates. I found no MLO tool that watches non-occupant co-borrower loans for the release moment, plans the release path by program, and treats the co-borrower as a separate client with their own next loan and their own consent and privacy. Release-of-liability specifics are from model knowledge and need checking against current FHA and VA servicing guidance.
- **Compliance design:** ECOA and Reg B: no inference of family or marital relationship; only the file's role flags; never a ranking input. Each person is a separate consumer for consent (TCPA), privacy (GLBA: no sharing one person's NPI with the other without authorization) and the D3 gate. HPPA: no credit data; income comes from the borrower. TRID: the update never forms an application until the borrower applies. Reg Z: no pay difference between a release refinance and anything else. M8 never calls a solo estimate an approval and never quotes a rate outside a priced result (APR and not-a-commitment line). AI disclosure first. Licensing: each person's new loan only in the MLO's licensed property states, otherwise the borrower-chosen handoff. No lead sold; the co-borrower is introduced only by their own consent.
- **Moat:** Each closed loan with a co-borrower on the platform becomes two future relationships with separate, provable consent. The rules table is shared across members. It also showcases the platform's multi-party consent model, which outside CRMs built around one contact per loan can't easily copy.
- **Reviews:** keep 7/10: The design mostly holds up. (1) Separate GLBA consent for co-borrowers on the same account is stricter than Reg P requires, which is fine. But showing the co-borrower card to the MLO as 'may want their own financing' is a marketing inference, and outreach to the co-borrower needs that person's own PEWC, gathered independently. The co-borrower must never be contacted because the occupant borrower suggested it. (2) The 'solo-qualification estimate' is a prequalification. If it is shown as 'likely' and the borrower then applies and is denied, there is UDAAP risk, and Reg B adverse-action duties arise only on an application. That is fine, but the wording must not imply approval. (3) Release of liability under FHA and VA (38 U.S.C. 3713) is decided by the servicer. The tool must not imply that the MLO can obtain it. Redesign: Show the co-borrower card only after the co-borrower has independently opted in (their own consent passport entry). Never prompt the occupant borrower to introduce the co-borrower for marketing. Label the estimate 'not a prequalification or approval' and route release requests only to the servicer. / keep 5/10: No known CRM watches non-occupant co-borrower loans for the release moment or treats the co-borrower as a separate client with separate consent. CRMs merge them into one household. Novelty holds, but the population is small and release of liability is done by the servicer. The solo-qualification estimate risks being read as pre-qualification, so it needs careful wording. Redesign: Build it as a trigger on Verified Loan Facts role flags plus a two-consent template, not a desk. Reuse the Guideline Ledger for the program release rules and Reuse Ledger for costs. Ship after the core desks. / drop 2/10: Low frequency: non-occupant co-borrower loans are a small share of a book, and few reach a release moment in any year. Conventional loans need a full refinance, which is an ordinary refi the MLO already handles. FHA and VA release of liability runs through the servicer, not the MLO. The 'second card' for the co-borrower's next loan is speculative. The build is not trivial (separate consent records and per-field authorization) for minimal weekly use. Redesign: Drop as a product. Keep the multi-party consent model (separate consent and GLBA rights per co-borrower) as a platform rule in D2 contacts, and let the borrower-initiated case go through the normal refi card.
- **Sources:** Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/PATCHLOG.md D2 (consent per contact, channel and purpose), D3, Repo: /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md idea 6 (Time Capsule as channel), HUD Handbook 4000.1 (non-occupant co-borrowers; servicing and release of liability): https://www.hud.gov/program_offices/housing/sfh/handbook_4000-1 (not opened; MED), VA release of liability: https://www.benefits.va.gov/homeloans/ (not opened; MED), 12 CFR 1002 (Reg B): https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1002, 16 CFR 313 / Reg P privacy: https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-12/chapter-X/part-1016


## Value Gap Desk: appraisal reconsideration with agent-supplied comps and a hard appraiser-independence firewall (round 2, high impact, ~4 weeks)

**When an appraisal comes in under the contract price, Command Center runs the program's reconsideration of value (ROV) process or VA's Tidewater window. It pulls in the agents' comparable sales, screens them, drafts the borrower's request in the lender's format and tracks the deadline. No message to the appraiser or the AMC can leave the platform.**

- **Problem:** A low appraisal is one of the most common reasons a purchase stalls or falls apart, and it is the moment agents judge a loan officer hardest. Both agents have comparable sales, but they usually end up in an MLO's personal email and get forwarded in whatever form. Outside the lender's ROV channel, a frustrated message to an appraiser can count as coercion under Reg Z's appraiser-independence rule. Broker MLOs also juggle different ROV forms, comp limits and deadlines at each wholesale lender and program (conventional, FHA, VA). The Tidewater window is short and is often missed.
- **How it works:** DATA: the appraisal report (the borrower gets a copy under Reg B 1002.14), the contract price, the program, and the wholesale lender's ROV form and rules from a per-lender reference table. It also uses comps that the listing and buyer agents upload through a secure link for this one file, using their own MLS access.
TRIGGER: any one of three events. The LOS appraisal-received milestone (ARIVE via Zapier) shows a value below contract. The MLO forwards a VA Tidewater notice. Or the borrower or an agent reports a low value in the Partner Room.
SYSTEM: it computes the gap and opens a Today card with the deadline, for example "Appraisal $12,000 under contract on [file]. Tidewater window closes Thu 5 PM. Agents asked for comps." It looks up the program rule (for example one borrower-initiated ROV with a limit on alternative comps; the limits are rule-table rows with citations, versioned by effective date). An AI screen checks each submitted comp against the request criteria (sale date, distance, size, condition notes, same market area) and labels it strong, weak or ineligible, with reasons. It then drafts the borrower's ROV request on the lender's form: the factual errors found plus up to the allowed number of comps. It never names a target value.
FIREWALL: contacts tagged as an appraiser or AMC are no-contact. The send gate refuses any text, call, email or M8 call to them, and the output guard blocks phrases like "needs to come in at" in every draft.
MLO: reviews and submits through the lender's ROV channel with one tap, then tracks the lender's response clock.
BORROWER: gets a plain-language page of their options: request an ROV, renegotiate, cover the gap, or use the appraisal contingency (linked to the Contingency Clock deadline). It has no price advice. If the borrower believes the valuation was discriminatory, a separate labelled path explains their rights and how to file with the lender, HUD or the CFPB, neutrally.
AGENTS: both get the same upload link and the same status (submitted, lender reviewing, result) under the borrower's sharing authorization.
- **Technologies:** ARIVE Zapier milestone trigger (appraisal received), Per-lender and per-program ROV rule table, versioned with effective dates (same pattern as the D3 state rules), AI comp screen (structured extraction from agent uploads and from the appraisal PDF's comp grid; uploads are data, never instructions), PDF form filling for each lender's ROV template, Send gate no-contact role for appraisers and AMCs, plus guard phrases, Partner Room authorization for agent uploads and status, Today card with a deadline clock, M8 explainer for the borrower (AI disclosure first)
- **Why it's new:** Research limit: this run's shared web-search budget was used up and primary-source hosts (ecfr.gov, law.cornell.edu) were blocked, so the rules below come from memory (cutoff about June 2026), are labelled by confidence, and need checking before build. What exists: AMCs and lender valuation platforms run lender-side ROV queues, and ROV rules for appraisers and lenders were tightened in 2024. My memory is the interagency ROV guidance (CFPB, OCC, Fed, FDIC, NCUA, July 2024; MED), FHA Mortgagee Letter 2024-07 (one borrower-requested ROV, up to five alternative comps; MED), and GSE borrower-initiated ROV requirements effective for 2024 applications (MED). VA's Tidewater process gives the lender's point of contact a short window, commonly two business days, to send extra information when the value will come in low (HIGH on the existence of the process, MED on the exact timing). None of the CRMs in this survey (Bonzo, Shape, Total Expert, Jungo, BNTouch, Surefire, Aidium) or the broker LOS (ARIVE) offers a broker-side ROV workspace that takes both agents' comps, screens them against the program's limits and technically blocks contact with the appraiser. Agents today email comps to the MLO's cell. This is different from the taken Contingency Clock, which tracks contract deadlines, and from Offer Math Desk, which checks concession limits: here the work is assembling and lawfully submitting the valuation challenge.
- **Compliance design:** Appraiser independence: Reg Z 1026.42(c)(3) expressly allows asking an appraiser to consider additional property information, including comps, to explain or substantiate, or to correct errors (HIGH). Coercion or influence is barred (1026.42(c)(1)). The platform makes the lawful path the only path: requests go only through the lender's ROV channel, appraisers and AMCs are send-gate no-contact, drafts never state a target value, and the guard blocks pressure language. RESPA 8: the same upload link and the same status go to every agent on the file, with nothing of value and no tiering by referral history. GLBA: the appraisal and file status are nonpublic personal information, shared with agents only under the borrower's revocable, logged Partner Room authorization. Comps the agents upload stay in that file's tenant and are deleted when the file closes or ends. MLS: the agent submits their own MLS data for the transaction under their own license, and Command Center never pulls MLS data itself [COUNSEL: confirm local MLS rules allow sharing comps with the lender for an ROV]. Fair lending: the same process and the same screen for every borrower. The bias-claim path is offered to everyone in neutral wording, and tract demographics are never inputs to the comp screen. TCPA: service messages on an active file, through the D3 gate. AI: M8 discloses that it is AI before explaining options and gives no pricing or valuation opinions. Copy: no success-rate claims ("ROVs usually win" is banned).
- **Moat:** The per-lender and per-program ROV reference table (form versions, comp limits, submission channels, effective dates) is shared reference data that every member's use keeps current. It is not lender performance data: cross-company lender ratings were rejected on antitrust grounds and stay rejected. Agents who see one MLO run a clean ROV ask for that MLO on the next file, and every MLO on the network offers the same tool.
- **Reviews:** keep 6/10: Value Gap Desk is lawful in concept: Reg Z 1026.42(c)(3)(i) permits asking for consideration of additional comparable information, and the AIR and FHA ML 2024-07 limit is one borrower ROV with up to five alternative comps (from memory, unverified). But this idea, ROV Desk and Appraisal Desk are three versions of the same feature. The compliance-sensitive differences are these. (1) An AI screen that labels comps and decides which ones go into the submission, run by a broker paid only if the loan closes, means the interested party is selecting evidence. The 2024 interagency ROV guidance expects the ROV to be borrower-initiated. If the MLO drafts it and submits it with one tap, it is effectively MLO-initiated. (2) VA Tidewater responses go through the lender's designated POC, not the broker. (3) Whether agents may share MLS comps with a lender for an ROV depends on local MLS rules, and some limit sharing to clients. Redesign: Merge with ROV Desk and Appraisal Desk into one Appraisal Desk. Make the ROV borrower-initiated and borrower-signed, with the borrower's reasons in their own words. The AI may flag factual errors and comp-eligibility facts, but the borrower or agent picks the comps. Route Tidewater through the lender's POC. Get MLS-rule confirmation per market before the agent upload link is turned on. / keep 6/10: Lender and AMC ROV intake already exists (Reggora, ICE Mercury Network, Clear Capital and the AMCs all added borrower-ROV workflows after the 2024 interagency guidance). Lender-side AI appraisal review (UAD checks from Cotality, Veros and Clear Capital) also exists. Training knowledge only; reggora.com was blocked. The broker-side assembly with both agents' comps, screening against program limits, and a technical block on contacting the appraiser is not offered by broker CRMs or ARIVE as far as known. Three near-duplicates are in this batch: this one, ROV Desk and Appraisal Desk. Redesign: Keep this as the single appraisal workspace and merge into it the UAD 3.6 factual check from ROV Desk and the ECOA notice and copy-delivery clock from Appraisal Desk. Submit through the lender's existing ROV portal or channel rather than building a new submission format. / drop 5/10: This duplicates ROV Desk and Appraisal Desk in the same batch. Three ideas for the same workflow must merge into one. Economically, low appraisals on purchases happen often enough to matter for purchase-heavy MLOs, but brokers can only route ROVs through the wholesale lender's channel, and reported ROV success rates are low. That caps the value at time saved assembling the package. The no-contact firewall and comp screen are good, cheap features. Redesign: Merge into Appraisal Desk (the most complete version: ECOA copy clock, value-gap options, borrower-initiated ROV). Carry over this idea's agent comp upload link and send-gate no-contact tag for appraisers and AMCs.
- **Sources:** https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1026/42/ (Reg Z appraiser independence; not opened this session), https://www.consumerfinance.gov/rules-policy/regulations/1002/14/ (Reg B appraisal copies; not opened), https://www.hud.gov/sites/dfiles/OCHCO/documents/2024-07hsgml.pdf (FHA ML 2024-07 ROV; URL pattern from memory, not opened), https://selling-guide.fanniemae.com/ (GSE borrower-initiated ROV requirements; not opened), https://www.benefits.va.gov/WARMS/pam26_7.asp (VA Lenders Handbook ch. 10, Tidewater; not opened), /home/user/RATEM8/docs/work/command-center-ideas-2026-11-18.md (rejected Lender Pulse, antitrust)


## Confidential Intro Rail: introductions from CPAs, attorneys and financial advisors that respect each profession's own confidentiality law (round 2, high impact, ~3 weeks)

**A CPA, divorce attorney or financial advisor never hands over a client's details. They give the client a link, and the client introduces themselves, chooses their licensed MLO, gives their own consent and decides what the professional may see back. Each profession gets a counsel-reviewed pack and a compliance receipt for its own files.**

- **Problem:** The default "I'll give your number to my mortgage guy" can break four laws at once. A tax preparer disclosing tax-return information without a prior signed consent risks IRC 7216 (a criminal misdemeanor) and IRC 6713 civil penalties. A lawyer needs client consent under Model Rule 1.6, and Rule 7.2 limits referral arrangements. A registered investment adviser or broker-dealer is under Reg S-P, with a 2024 amendment, and must disclose referral conflicts. The MLO receives a number with no TCPA consent for the MLO, which is the provenance gap D2 puts on hold. The high-trust professionals who could send an MLO steady, well-documented business are exactly those who face real penalties, so many quietly don't refer at all.
- **How it works:** DATA: the professional's free partner profile (name, firm, profession, state; no fee, no account tier), the profession pack (wording, required consent forms, what may and may not flow), and the MLO's licensing from lib/routing.ts.
TRIGGER: the professional shares a link or QR code from their own phone or email. Command Center never contacts the client first.
CLIENT: lands on a page that says plainly that no information came from their CPA, attorney or advisor. If M8 helps, it discloses that it is AI first. The client enters the property state and their own details, picks a loan officer (shown in random order where several are licensed; the client's existing MLO comes first, as the existing Homecoming design does), and grants consent per channel and purpose in the consent passport. They also choose what the professional may see back: nothing, "connected", or milestones only.
CPA PACK: if the client later wants their CPA to send returns or a profit-and-loss statement, the system generates a 7216 consent in the mandatory Rev. Proc. 2013-14 wording. The CPA has it signed before any disclosure, and the upload goes into the file. Until that consent is signed, the CPA upload slot stays locked.
ATTORNEY PACK: the link and receipt say the introduction is non-exclusive and that nothing of value flows. Command Center never offers or records a reciprocal referral agreement.
ADVISOR PACK: the client's own submission is a consumer-directed disclosure. The advisor gets a receipt for their books and records, and a conflict line ("no compensation is paid to your advisor") they can file.
MLO: a Today card such as "New introduction via a CPA firm: client chose you, consented to service texts, property in WA."
PROFESSIONAL: sees only what the client allowed, plus a downloadable receipt: date, what was shared, consent text, and a statement that nothing of value was paid.
- **Technologies:** Partner link and QR generator (no partner fee or tiers), Consent passport, per channel and purpose, with E-SIGN capture, Profession packs as versioned config, counsel-reviewed (CPA/7216, attorney/Rule 1.6 and 7.2, RIA and broker-dealer/Reg S-P, CFP Board conflict disclosure), Rev. Proc. 2013-14 consent generator with e-signature by the client, before any CPA upload unlocks, lib/routing.ts licensing check and random-order chooser, PDF and JSON compliance receipts, KMS-signed, D2 provenance tag 'introduced by professional, self-submitted' (proof-grade consent, never on hold)
- **Why it's new:** Research limit: web search was exhausted and primary hosts were blocked this run; the legal rules are from memory and labelled. Existing tools are referral-partner portals in mortgage CRMs (Total Expert partner management, which was 'coming soon' in April 2026 release notes per the survey; Bonzo and Shape partner sharing). They pass the client's contact details from the partner to the loan officer, so the borrower's consent and the partner's confidentiality are the partner's problem. I found no mortgage CRM in the survey that models the professional's own law (IRC 7216, Model Rules 1.6 and 7.2, Reg S-P, the AICPA's referral-fee disclosure rule ET 1.520) or gives the professional a receipt for their own compliance file. The taken ideas are adjacent but different. The Pass-Along Card covers past clients passing the MLO along without collecting a friend's number. The Partner Room covers borrower-directed sharing on a live file. The CPA Income Desk covers analysis of a return the borrower brings. This idea is the inbound introduction from a regulated professional, designed so that the professional discloses nothing and has proof of that.
- **Compliance design:** RESPA 8: nothing of value flows to any professional, in any direction, ever. There are no partner tiers, no per-introduction counts shown to the MLO as a 'partner score', and the spend firewall (next idea) cannot read the introduction source. TCPA: consent comes from the client directly for the named MLO, so the gap where consent given to the CPA doesn't transfer never arises. The professional never texts on the platform's behalf; they share a link from their own device as a personal act [COUNSEL: confirm the share isn't treated as the MLO's message]. IRC 7216 and 6713: no tax-return information moves before a signed consent in the mandatory form (HIGH on the requirement; Rev. Proc. 2013-14 wording to be confirmed). Attorneys: no reciprocal agreement is created; the non-exclusive statement is shown [COUNSEL: state bar variants]. Reg S-P: a consumer-directed disclosure, and receipts support the adviser's records. GLBA: borrower data stays in the chosen MLO's tenant, and what the professional sees back is borrower-controlled and revocable. Licensing: only loan officers licensed in the property state are offered; otherwise the page says so (Relay applies). Fair lending: the chooser's order is random, never ranked by any borrower attribute. AI disclosure precedes any M8 interaction. Copy: no 'referral fee' and no 'commission', per check:copy.
- **Moat:** Each profession pack, once reviewed by counsel and accepted by a state CPA society, bar ethics hotline or broker-dealer compliance team, becomes the safe default those professionals tell each other about. More MLOs on the network means more states and property locations where a professional's client can always find a licensed MLO. That is the coverage a single MLO, or a lender's closed funnel, can't give a CPA with clients in five states.
- **Reviews:** keep 8/10: This is a strong design. IRC 7216 and Rev. Proc. 2013-14 require the mandatory consent wording before a preparer discloses or uses return information, and the locked slot respects that. Three residual risks. (1) If the MLO gives the professional a pre-written message or a bulk-share template, the professional's sends can be treated as made 'on behalf of' the MLO under the TCPA (FCC vicarious liability, the 2013 Dish Network ruling). (2) Attorneys who act as closing or settlement attorneys are settlement service providers, so any later reciprocal business creates RESPA 8 'understanding' risk even with no money. (3) A FINRA-registered advisor's firm may treat referrals as an outside business activity (Rule 3270) even unpaid, which is the firm's issue, but the receipt should not claim to satisfy it. Redesign: Give professionals only a link or QR code, never message templates for mass sending. Add a rule that the platform tracks no reciprocal flow, for example no MLO-to-attorney introductions are counted or surfaced. Word the advisor and CPA receipts as factual records, not as compliance certifications. / keep 5/10: Partner portals in Total Expert, Bonzo and Shape pass contact details from partner to LO. None that I know of models the professional's own confidentiality law or gives the professional a receipt. Tax software (Drake, ProConnect and others) already generates IRC 7216 consents, so the CPA pack's consent form is not new. Mechanically this is a self-intro link plus a consent page, close to the taken Pass-Along Card. Redesign: Build it on the Pass-Along Card and consent-passport plumbing with profession-specific copy. Start with the CPA pack only, because 7216 is the clearest rule. Get the 7216 consent from the CPA's own tax software rather than generating it. / keep 5/10: Cheap to build: it is a link or QR intake page on top of the consent passport. It also fixes a real consent gap, because consent given to a CPA doesn't transfer to the MLO. Economics are modest, since professional introductions are a small, low-velocity channel and CPAs rarely use a lender's software. The per-profession packs (IRC 7216 wording, bar rules, Reg S-P receipts) each need counsel time out of proportion to volume. The 7216 consent is the CPA's own obligation and the CPA's own software usually handles it. Redesign: MVP: one generic 'professional introduction' link with the client self-intake, the consent passport and an optional receipt. Add a profession-specific pack only after counsel review and real demand from members. Skip generating 7216 consents; tell the CPA it needs their own signed 7216 consent first and keep the upload slot locked.
- **Sources:** https://www.irs.gov/pub/irs-drop/rp-13-14.pdf (Rev. Proc. 2013-14, 7216 consent format; not opened), https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/26/301.7216-3 (consent before disclosure; host blocked this session), https://www.americanbar.org/groups/professional_responsibility/publications/model_rules_of_professional_conduct/ (Model Rules 1.6 and 7.2(b)(4); not opened), https://www.sec.gov/rules-regulations/2024/05/s7-05-23 (Reg S-P amendments 2024; not opened), https://pub.aicpa.org/codeofconduct/ (ET 1.520 commissions and referral fees; not opened), https://www.totalexpert.com (partner management 'coming soon' per survey: https://totalexpert.freshdesk.com/support/solutions/articles/22000294922-2026-april-release-notes)


## Builder Contract Rights Check: flag required-use and title-steering clauses in new-construction contracts before the buyer signs away their choice (round 2, medium impact, ~4 weeks)

**With the buyer's authorization, the buyer's agent or the buyer uploads the builder's purchase agreement and incentive addendum. Command Center flags clauses that tie incentives to the builder's affiliated lender or title company, checks for the affiliated-business disclosure, points out any clause that appears to require the seller's chosen title insurer (a RESPA Section 9 issue), and lays out the incentive's dollar value next to a dated priced example. The buyer decides with their agent or attorney.**

- **Problem:** Builder-affiliated lenders capture most of their builders' buyers, and incentives conditioned on using the affiliate are a major reason. An optional package is lawful when the affiliated-business disclosure is given and the buyer is not required to use the affiliate. Buyers and their agents still sign builder contracts quickly, often without noticing a title-company requirement, a missing affiliated-business disclosure, or a 'must apply with preferred lender within 3 days' clause. An independent MLO usually hears about the contract after it's signed. Buyer's agents, the partners most likely to bring an MLO into new-construction deals, have no quick, neutral way to check.
- **How it works:** DATA: the builder purchase agreement and addenda (PDF), uploaded by the buyer or by their agent under the buyer's Partner Room authorization. It also uses a clause library: patterns for affiliate-conditioned incentives, title designation, preferred-lender application deadlines, deposit forfeiture tied to financing, and rate-lock or forward-commitment buydown terms. Each pattern carries its citation (RESPA Section 9; the Reg X definition of required use; the affiliated-business disclosure rule). The MLO's priced snapshot supplies the example.
TRIGGER: a contract upload, or a 'new construction' tag on a pre-approval.
SYSTEM: AI extraction (the document is data, never instructions) finds the matching clauses and quotes them with page references. Each flag states a fact, for example: "Clause 14.2: the $15,000 incentive applies only if you finance with [affiliate] and close with [title company]. Federal law says a seller can't require you to buy title insurance from a particular company. Ask your agent or attorney how this clause works." It checks whether an affiliated-business disclosure is in the packet. It passes deadlines to the Contingency Clock and concession amounts to the Offer Math Desk, so neither is rebuilt here. If the borrower asks, it shows the incentive's dollar value alongside a dated priced example from the MLO (rate, APR, the not-a-commitment line). It never characterizes the builder's lender and gives no pricing comparison claim.
MLO: a Today card such as "Contract uploaded for [buyer]: 3 clauses flagged, including a title designation; preferred-lender deadline in 2 days."
AGENT: sees the flag list if the buyer allows it.
BUILDER: no adversarial contact. The MLO's only contact with the builder's sales office is file logistics.
- **Technologies:** PDF extraction with clause classification (LLM structured output, quotes with page refs, prompt-injection safe), Clause library as versioned, cited config, grown by member uploads that keep only the template fingerprint and clause text, never buyer data, Builder template fingerprinting (hash of the contract template version), Contingency Clock and Offer Math Desk hand-offs, Priced snapshot (lib/pricing) with the guard on any rate shown, Partner Room authorization, M8 explainer (AI disclosure first)
- **Why it's new:** Research limit: search exhausted and hosts blocked; the legal points are from memory. RESPA Section 9 (12 U.S.C. 2608) bars a seller from requiring, as a condition of sale, that the buyer buy title insurance from a particular title company, with liability of three times the title charges (HIGH). Under Reg X, offering an optional package or discount is not 'required use' when the buyer isn't forced to use the affiliate (HIGH on the concept; exact text to confirm). The affiliated-business disclosure is required when referring to an affiliate (1024.15; HIGH). Public builders report high affiliate mortgage capture rates in their 10-K filings (MED; check the current figures). What exists: builders' own sales-office software, and builder-affiliated lenders' portals. I found no tool for the buyer's agent or the independent MLO that reads a builder contract for required-use and Section 9 issues. It's adjacent to but distinct from the taken Builder Completion and Lock Desk (construction timing and lock extensions), the Offer Math Desk (concession limits) and the Contingency Clock (deadlines); this one checks the buyer's legal choice of lender and title company in the contract text.
- **Compliance design:** Unauthorized practice of law: the tool quotes clauses and cites statutes; it never interprets the contract or advises signing or not, and every flag ends with 'ask your agent or attorney' [COUNSEL: UPL review per state]. RESPA 8: nothing of value to the agent, and the flags are offered equally to any agent on a file. RESPA 9 and AfBA: stated as facts with citations, with no accusation against the builder.
